Changing an Employee’s Project

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Project_Change.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Project_Change.htm

Changing an Employee’s Project

Use the Project Change form to change an employee’s project. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To change an employee’s project:

  1. Enter the Employee by either typing the name or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records, or by clicking the field label to open the search window. The Current Employment information displays.

  2. Enter or select the New Project for the employee.

  3. Enter or select the Project Start Date when the new project goes into effect.

  4. Click Save.

Note: Note the following:

  • An error message displays if you enter a past date.

  • When the future date occurs, the system automatically makes the employee’s change in the Employee Details form and in the payroll that it generates after the change is effective. (That is, if the payroll that coincides with the effective date of the change has already been generated, the new values do not automatically update. However, when that payroll posts, the change occurs in the next payroll that is auto-generated.)

Processing Time Off Auto Register Enrollment

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/PTO_Auto_Register_Enrollment.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/PTO_Auto_Register_Enrollment.htm

Processing Time Off Auto Register Enrollment

The Time Off automatic enrollment process tests every employee against the eligibility rules (set up for the client in Time Off Auto Enroll Rules, found in the Client menu) and enrolls employees into absence benefits if they meet the eligibility criteria.

Use the Time Off Auto Register Enrollment form as a quick way to build up a list of employees in a plan and assign them their first Accrued Thru Date. You would typically use this feature when you need to enroll existing employees in a new Time Off plan.

[Image: screenshot]

To build up a list of employees in a plan and assign them:

  1. If appropriate, enter the Benefit Start Date to override the default.

The system uses this date to calculate the number of months that the employee has worked for the client. If you do not enter a date in the field, the system automatically calculates the date on which it would have begun for each employee.

Note: The Benefit Start Date field will not override on registers where the Time Off Auto-Enroll Rule has either Seniority Date or Hire Date selected on the Use Seniority Date or Hire/Rehire Date? field.

  1. In the Accrued Thru Date field, you should enter the date through which time off benefits would have previously been accrued.

  • When you enter a date, the system calculates each employee’s Time Off benefits from that date forward.

  • If you leave the date field blank, the system calculates each employee’s benefits from their date of hire through to today’s date. This is an extremely rare situation. Be sure that this is what you need to do, as it can impact payroll.

Read How PrismHR Uses the Accrued Thru Date for Time Off BenefitsHow PrismHR Uses the Accrued Thru Date for Time Off BenefitsHow PrismHR Uses the Accrued Thru Date for Time Off Benefits to learn more.

  1. Click Run.

The system enrolls eligible employees in the plan, then displays a count of the employees who were enrolled. It also displays a list of any employees who were not enrolled and why.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. When you finish reviewing the information, click Close to exit the form or Cancel to clear the form and use it to run another enrollment process.

How PrismHR Uses the Accrued Thru Date for Time Off Benefits

When it is time for payroll to send out paychecks, the system calculates Time Off benefits from the day after the last date through which the employee accrued benefits (the “accrued through” date) up through the period end date.

For example, employees in the ABC Company are paid semi-monthly and were last paid on May 15. When they are paid on June 1, the system knows the accrued through date is May 15, so it calculates the Time Off benefits from May 16 through June 1.

If your organization added ABC Company to PrismHR on May 20, you would want to automatically enroll the ABC employees in Time Off benefits. The system has never calculated payroll for these employees before, but its calculations require an “accrued through” date. You define the accrued through date as May 15, so the calculations result in a normal accrual cycle on June 1.

Therefore, you use the Time Off Auto Register Enrollment form to not only automatically enroll the employees, you also define an Accrued Thru Date date for the system. When payroll processes the paychecks, the system determines the employees’ Time Off benefits from the day after the date you entered.

Related Topics

Setting Up Time Off Plans

Specifying Time Off Auto-enroll Rules

========================================================================

Manually Enrolling Employees in Time Off Register

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/PTO_Register_Enrollment.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/PTO_Register_Enrollment.htm

Manually Enrolling Employees in Time Off Register

Use the Time Off Register Enrollment form to manually enroll employees in the Time Off register and set the starting hours.

Note: You can create a new enrollment even if an employee has an archived register.

Once you save the enrollment, PrismHR creates the register for the employee based on the accrual schedule and Time Off plan.

Note: Note: To make changes to the employee’s Time Off register values, see Adjusting Accrued or Carryover Hours for Employees

[Image: screenshot]

To manually enroll employees in a Time Off register:

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  2. Enter the Accrual Schedule used to track time off for this plan.

  3. Enter the Time Off Plan in which to enroll the employee.

Note: Note: PrismHR uses the Benefit Start Date to calculate the number of months the employee has worked for the client. The system uses this in conjunction with the rules defined for a Time Off plan to determine the number of hours the employee can earn during a given accrual period. The default is the employee’s last hire date, but you can change this.

  1. Enter the Benefit Start Date. The system uses this date to calculate the number of months that the employee has worked for the client. The default is the employee’s last hire date, but you can edit it as needed. See About Benefit Start Dates.

  2. Enter the Carryover Hours, which is the number of hours carried over from a previous accrual year. Typically, you would only enter this value when setting up the register for the first time.

  3. Enter the YTD Hours Calculated (+), which is the starting balance of Time Off hours accrued in the current year.

Note: If you enroll an employee in a Time Off plan with an initial YTD Hours Calculated (+) (accrual amount) that is greater than the Max Per Year in the Time Off plan, a warning message displays in the Time Off Register Enrollment form when you enter the Calculated Thru Date, “Warning: no additional hours will be accrued because the initial amount is greater than the maximum accrual of <number> hours set up in the Time Off plan.” To continue, click OK.

  1. Enter the YTD Hours Taken (-), which is the number of Time Off hours already used in the current year.

  2. Based on the entries, the YTD Hours Available (=) displays.

  3. Enter the Year End Date for the current year. The default comes from the plan’s setup.

  4. Enter the Calculated Thru Date, the date through which Time Off hours have been accrued. See Understanding the Calculated Thru Date Field for more information on entering the correct date.

  5. Enter the Carryover Expires On date when the available carryover hours expire, if applicable.

About Benefit Start Dates

The Benefit Start Date field dictates the calculation that the system uses.

  • You can only adjust the Benefit Start Date for employees enrolled in fiscal year registers. You cannot change it for anniversary-based registers.

  • When the eligibility for Time Off benefits is based on seniority level (the number of months worked), the system uses its calculation based on this date to determine the employee’s seniority level. For more information, see Defining Time Off Plans.

  • If the client’s Time Off register uses Anniversary Year End as the accrual schedule’s accrual calculation method, the system uses the date to calculate the Time Off register year-end date; see Creating Time Off Accrual Schedules.

Understanding the Calculated Thru Date Field

If the employee has accumulated Time Off hours prior to the initialization of this Time Off Register, you must enter the number of Time Off hours, any carryover hours available, and the date through which the hours were accrued.

WARNING: If you do not enter the correct date in the Calculated Thru Date field, PrismHR may accrue excess hours for this employee.

It is critical that you enter the correct “accrued through” date in the Calculated Thru Date field. The system accrues any Time Off hours from this date forward, according to the terms of the absence Time Off plan and accrual schedule. To determine the date correctly, it is important to understand the client’s Time Off plan; that is, does it accrue increments of Time Off hours throughout the year, or perhaps deposit the whole amount at one time?

For example, an employee has three vacation days available to her at the time you take on a particular client. The employee is not scheduled to accrue any more until January 1 of the following year. You must ensure the date you enter in the Calculated Thru Date field is December 31. The next time the register is triggered, whether the it is the next pay period, end of month, or another time, PrismHR will calculate the number of days between December 31 and the trigger date.

During payroll processing, PrismHR automatically updates the employee’s Time Off register with any due accruals.

[Image: screenshot]

If you are going to update Time Off hours more frequently, such as when increments of time are deposited each payroll period, ensure you understand exactly what “accrued through” date the client used when they submitted the employee’s Time Off hours to you. Was it through the last day of the payroll period or through the pay date?

You must determine the “accrued through” date with the client to ensure the employee’s Time Off register accrues correctly.

Reactivating an Employee

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Reactivation.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Reactivation.htm

Reactivating an Employee

You can reactivate an employee who was on a leave of absence using the Reactivation form.

Note: All the fields in this form are mandatory, so make sure you complete all the fields before saving the record.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: The PTO Accruals panel only displays when the following conditions are met:

  • The entered or selected employee is enrolled in one or more PTO registers.

  • You enter a Reactivation Date that is either the current day or a date before that day.

To reactivate an employee, do the following:

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window. The employee’s current status and type display.

  2. Select the Reactivated Employment Status.

Note: This field displays the full description of the status code, separated by a dash (-) in front of the status code.

  1. Select the Reactivated Employment Type.

Note: This field displays the full description of the type code, separated by a dash (-) in front of the type code.

  1. Enter the Reactivation Date.

  2. Select the Reason Code the best describes the employee reactivation.

  3. To resume PTO accruals for the employee, deselect the Stop PTO Accruals field for the PTO Register Type. (The PTO Register Type and PTO Benefit Plan columns display the PTO Register and Plan where the employee is enrolled.)

Note: After you save this selection and there is a reactivation approval policy in place, when the reactivation is approved this setting registers in the Stop PTO Accruals field on the Employee Time Off Register form.

  1. Click Save.

Note: Note: To perform an employment reactivation for multiple employees at once. See Processing Mass Employee Changes.

Reviewing Status Type History

You can access the employee’s status history by opening the Actions menu and selecting Status Type History. See Viewing Employment Status Updates for Employees for more information.

Rehiring a Terminated Employee

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Rehire.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Rehire.htm

Rehiring a Terminated Employee

You can rehire an employee who was previously terminated using the Rehire form.

When the Rehire form opens, four panels display the following information after you enter or select a terminated employee:

  • Rehire: Displays the employee’s employment status information.

  • Personal Information: Displays the employee’s contact information.

  • Employment Details: Displays the employee’s work details.

  • Pay Details: Displays the employee’s pay information.

Note: Any changes you make in the Employment Details panel and the Pay Details panel take effect immediately and do not abide by the date entered in the Rehire Date field in the Rehire panel. (Future dated changes are not supported. If you enter a future date, the system uses the current date.)

After you enter or select an employee, the following panels display depending on the client’s settings on the Client Details form:

  • E-Verification Options: Select whether to launch E-Verification for this employee. (This panel only displays if the Enable E-Verification? field in the Control tab on the Client Details form is enabled and the employee’s worksite location is not selected to exclude E-Verify.)

  • Onboarding Options: Select whether to launch Onboarding for this employee. (This panel only displays if the Electronic On-Boarding for Rehires field in the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to Prompt.)

  • Benefits Enrollment Options: Select whether to launch Benefits Enrollment for this employee. (This panel only displays if the Electronic Enrollment field in the Control tab on the Client Details form is set to NextGen.)

Note: Note: To rehire multiple employees at once, see Processing Mass Employee Changes.

For example, after you enter or select a terminated employee’s information, the following panels might display:

Note: Required fields are noted in red on this form. In addition, required field settings in the Control tab on the Client Details form that have associated settings in the Controls panel on the New Hire form and the Employee Details form for Department, Shift, Work Group, or Division fields are conditionally required on the Rehire form.

Setting a New Hire Reporting Date

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Set%20New%20Hire%20Reporting%20Date.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Set New Hire Reporting Date.htm

Setting a New Hire Reporting Date

Use the Set New Hire Reporting Dates process to enter new values for the new hire reporting dates. When a new employee is hired, the employer is required to report that new hire to the state they are hired in via an electronic file (Back Office) > Operations|Action > New Hire Reporting. The final step of this reporting download process is to add the new hire reporting date on the employee record so that the employee is not reported multiple times.

The Set New Hire Reporting Date option enables you to manually add the new hire reporting date on the employee record in either of the following situations:

  • The employee record was locked, therefore not included in the New Hire Reporting Download file, but was manually added to the States Download.

  • The employer does not want to report the new hire to the state using the New Hire Reporting Download file.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To set new hire reporting dates:

  1. In the Work Centers panel, under Compliance click New Hire and Termination Reporting. Under Reporting Date Overrides select Set New Hire Reporting Date.

  2. Starting New Hire Report Date is a required date field — select the start date of reporting date range.

  3. Ending New Hire Report Date is a required date field — select the end date of reporting date range.

  4. Click the Client ID link to select a specific client for the report or leave blank to select all clients.

  5. Click the Employee ID link to select a specific employee for the report or leave blank to select all employees.

  6. Click Process. A progress window opens that shows the number of records read and the number of records updated and notifies you when the process is complete. The Close and Report buttons remain disabled while the report is being generated.

  7. Click Report to display the Job Control Status Report window.

  8. In the Job Control Status Report window, click the XLS link to download the report.

Changing an Employee’s Work Shift

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Shift_Change.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Shift_Change.htm

Changing an Employee’s Work Shift

Use the Shift Change form to change an employee’s work shift. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To change an employee’s work shift:

  1. Enter the Employee by either typing the name or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records, or by clicking the field label to open the search window. The Current Employment information displays.

  2. Enter or select the New Work Shift for the employee.

  3. Enter or select the Work Shift Start Date when the new work shift goes into effect.

  4. Click Save.

Note: Note the following:

  • An error message displays if you enter a past date.

  • When the future date occurs, the system automatically makes the employee’s change in the Employee Details form and in the payroll that it generates after the change is effective. (That is, if the payroll that coincides with the effective date of the change has already been generated, the new values do not automatically update. However, when that payroll posts, the change occurs in the next payroll that is auto-generated.)

Performing Status Type Changes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Status_Type_Change.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Status_Type_Change.htm

Performing Status Type Changes

Use the Status Type Change form to change:

  • Employment status to one of the same class, such as Active PT to Active FT

  • Employment type, such as seasonal to part-time

  • Employment hours

[Image: screenshot]

Note: To perform a status change for multiple employees at once, see Processing Mass Employee Changes.

To change an employee status, do the following:

  1. Enter or select the Employee ID. The employee’s name, current employment status and type display.

Note: A warning message displays when there is an existing future dated pay history record for the same effective date and an entry exists in the New Standard Hours field, “A Pay Rate Change has already been entered with that effective date. If you continue, that change will be discarded. Do you wish to continue?” If you select Yes, the system saves the record and clears the form. (Selecting No returns you to the form where the entries still display.)

  1. Select the New Employment Status. This must be a status of the same class as the current status: either active, leave, or terminated. If you need to change the status from an on leave status to an active status, for example, you need to use the Reactivation feature.

Note: When you perform a status type change for an employee who has an on leave status classification or a terminated status classification, the following items do not update:

  • Auto enrollment of benefits

  • Auto enrollment of PTO

  • Auto enrollment of retirement

  • Standard hours or default time sheet hours updates

  1. Select the New Employment Type associated with an updated employment status.

  2. Enter the New Standard Hours associated with an updated employment status. The Current column displays the employee’s current Standard Hours and Default T/S Hours (if any).

  3. Enter the New Default T/S Hours associated with an updated employment status.

Note: The New Standard Hours and New Default T/S Hours field values are not required, so only enter these hours if they are applicable. Also, to hide these fields so Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors cannot access them (recommended method), set the field security to Hidden for the form. (Setting the field security to Disable disables the fields.)

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Status/Type Date when the status change goes into effect.

  2. Select the Reason that best describes this change.

  3. Click Save.

Note: Note: If the status will terminate the employee, and the employee ID is associated with a user account, then the user loses access to the client. If the user needs temporary access to the client information, the user administrator needs to adjust the Data Security. See Granting User Access to Former Clients and Employees.

Actions Menu

You can access related features by selecting them from the Actions menu.

  • Reason Codes: see Creating Reason Codes

  • Status Type History: see Viewing Employment Status Updates for Employees

Creating Records for Terminated Employees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Terminated%20Take-On%20Employee%20Entry.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Terminated Take-On Employee Entry.htm

Creating Records for Terminated Employees

Use the Terminated Take-On Employee Entry form to create records for terminated employees before the client was onboarded. Other processes such as Employment Information Report (Form EEO-1) and COBRA also utilize this information.

Note: Note:

  • Uses Client Access Group and employee level security.

  • Worksite Managers, Worksite Trusted Advisors, and Service Providers can access the form.

Note: Note: This form cannot be found on the HRP.ADMIN side.

To access the form, click HR. Under HR|Action click Terminated Take-On Employee Entry.

To create the record of the terminated take on employee:

  1. Complete the following fields as required:

In this field — Enter this

Social Security Number — A valid Social Security Number.

First Name — The first name of the employee.

Middle Name — The middle name of the employee. This field is optional.

Last Name — The last name of the employee.

Birth Date — The date of birth of the employee. This field is optional.

Address Line 1 — The street address of the employee.

Address Line 2 — Additional address information of the employee example apartment number. This field is optional.

Zip — The five digit zip code of the employee. Select the appropriate option from the Geocode Selection - Zip Code window if more than one GeoCode is associated with the zip code.

City — Auto populates based on zip code.

State — Auto populates based on zip code.

GeoCode — Auto populates based on zip code or selection from the Geocode Selection - Zip Code window if more than one GeoCode is associated with the zip code.

Employee ID — Auto populates the Employee ID assigned by the system.

Employer — The employer code of the employee or click the Employer link to select the employer code.

Employment Status — The employment status code of the employee or click the Employment Status link to select the employment status code.

Employment Type — The employment type code of the employee or click the Employment Type link to select the employment type code.

Position Code — The position code of the employee or click the Position Code link to select the position code.

Worksite Location — The worksite location code of the employee or click the Worksite Location link to select the worksite location code.

Department Code — The department code of the employee or click the Department Code link to select the department code. This field is optional.

Division Code — The division code of the employee or click the Division Code link to select the division code. This field is optional.

Project/Cost Center — The project/cost center code of the employee or click the Project/Cost Center link to select the project/cost center code. This field is optional.

Benefit Group — The benefit group code of the employee of click the Benefit Group link to select the benefit group code. This field is optional.

  1. Click Save to create the record of the terminated employee. The screen is refreshed.

========================================================================

Terminating an Employee

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Termination.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Termination.htm

Terminating an Employee

Use the Employee Termination form to terminate an employee.

Note: Note: To terminate multiple employees at once, see Processing Mass Employee Changes.

When you terminate an employee, PrismHR performs the following based on your system’s global settings:

  • Terminates the employee’s benefits

  • Removes the employee’s web access login

  • Disables the employee’s ACH

  • Refunds the client for the new hire fee, if the PEO has a policy when new hires are terminated within 30 days of being hired

  • Displays a warning flag if the employee has an outstanding loan balance

[Image: screenshot]

To terminate an employee:

  1. Complete the following information:
Field Description
Employee Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window. The employee’s name, Current Position, Current Employment Status, and Current Employment Type display. Note: If you terminate an employee who is both a PTO approver (as assigned on the Employee Details form) with assigned reports and a worksite manager, when you click Save a message displays that prompts you to review the PTO Approver Reassignment form, “<Employee ID> was assigned to approve PTO. Would you like to reassign them to a new PTO Approver now?” Selecting Yes displays the PTO Approver Reassignment form, where you can reassign those employees to a new PTO approver. (See Processing PTO Approver Reassignments.)
Termination Status Code Select the Termination Status Code. Note: This field displays the full description of the status code, separated by a dash (-) in front of the status code.
Reason Code Select the Reason Code for termination.
Termination Date Enter the Termination Date. Note: Benefits for employees who have been terminated end on either the 15th of the month or at the end of the month, depending on the Termination Date. If the Termination Date is on or before the 15th of the month, benefits terminate on the 15th. If the Termination Date is after 15th of the month, benefits terminate at the end of the month.
Okay to Rehire Use the Okay to Rehire drop-down to indicate whether the employee could potentially be rehired. If you select Yes or Not Specified, the employee will be available in the Rehire form’s search. - Yes: The employee can be rehired. - No: The employee should not be rehired. - Not Specified: There is no preference specified as to whether the employee could be rehired.
  • Yes: The employee can be rehired.
  • No: The employee should not be rehired.
  • Not Specified: There is no preference specified as to whether the employee could be rehired. | Last Pay Period Hours | Enter the number of hours for the last payout to the terminated employee. (Entering an amount here populates the timesheet for this terminated employee’s last pay period, which is the pay period where their termination date is entered.) > Note: Note the following: - You can enter positive numbers greater than zero (0) or partial hours up to two decimal places. - The hours entered here only applies to regularly scheduled payrolls and will not display on manual or special payrolls. - If the termination date is outside of the current pay period, the system applies the hours entered to the last pay period. |
  • You can enter positive numbers greater than zero (0) or partial hours up to two decimal places.
  • The hours entered here only applies to regularly scheduled payrolls and will not display on manual or special payrolls.
  • If the termination date is outside of the current pay period, the system applies the hours entered to the last pay period. | If ACH, Turn Off ACH? | Select If ACH, Turn Off ACH if the employee should not receive further ACH transactions. The system sets the employee’s ACH status to Inactive. | | | Last Day Worked | Enter the Last Day Worked, which is the last date on which the employee worked. | | | Provider Notified On | Enter the Provider Notified On, which is the day on which the service provider was notified that employment was terminated. | | | Reassign Pending Approvals? | Use the Reassign Pending Approvals? drop-down to indicate whether the pending approvals should be reassigned to another user: - Yes: Reassigns pending approvals to other users - No: Termination processes without reassigning pending approvals to other users Selecting Yes enables the Reassign to User ID field. Click the link to select a user from the Customer Users with Access search list, whose users are configured in the Client Security form on the Client | Change menu. Note: If you do not check Reassign Pending Approvals? and then click Save a message displays, “This employee has pending approvals assigned to them. Do you want to reassign them to a different client user?” Click Yes to reassign this employee’s pending approvals. |
  • Yes: Reassigns pending approvals to other users
  • No: Termination processes without reassigning pending approvals to other users | Termination Explanation | Enter a detailed Termination Explanation, up to 200 characters, regarding the employee’s termination. Note: You can enter a Termination Explanation that exceeds 200 characters when you enable the TERMEXPLAIN custom feature code on the System Parameters form. |
  1. If the employee has an available balance in their PTO Register, that information displays in the Unpaid PTO Payout panel. To apply the available hours to the employee’s payout, click Yes in the Add hours to current payroll? field. The system adds the available hours to the current timesheet.

Note: You can select the tool tip icon

[Image: screenshot]

in the Add hours to current payroll? field to display information on how to use this field.

To hide this panel so Worksite Managers and Worksite Trusted Advisors cannot access it (recommended method), set the field security to Hidden for the form. (Setting the field security to Disable disables the panel on this form.)

[Image: screenshot]

  1. If applicable, enter COBRA information:
a. Indicate whether the system should create a COBRA participant record.
b. Select the COBRA Qualifying Event.
— —
c. Enter the COBRA Qualifying Event Date.
— —
  1. Click Save.

Important!: After terminating an employee, you must also deactivate that employee by unchecking the Active User field on the Users form. (Terminated, yet active, employees will continue to display on the SuperBatch form.)

Status Type History

You can access the employee’s status history by opening the Actions menu and selecting Status Type History. See Viewing Employment Status Updates for Employees for more information.

Changing an Employee’s Work Group

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Work_Group_Change.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Work_Group_Change.htm

Changing an Employee’s Work Group

Use the Work Group Change form to change an employee’s work group. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To change an employee’s work group:

  1. Enter the Employee by either typing the name or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records, or by clicking the field label to open the search window. The Current Employment information displays.

  2. Enter or select the New Work Group for the employee.

  3. Enter or select the Work Group Start Date when the new work group goes into effect.

  4. Click Save.

Note: An error message displays if you enter a past date.

Setting Up Workers’ Compensation Cases

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Workers_Compensation_Cases.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Workers_Compensation_Cases.htm

Setting Up Workers’ Compensation Cases

Track detailed information about a workers’ compensation case using the Workers’ Compensation Cases form.

Workers’ Compensation Case

Use the Workers’ Compensation Case panel to review or add a new case.

To set up basic Worker’s Compensation information:

  1. Enter an existing Case Number or click New Case to create a new case.

  2. Enter the Employee ID.

  3. Enter the Claim Date, which is the day the benefit claim was made.

  4. Enter the Claim Number assigned by the insurance company.

  5. Enter the Jurisdiction, which is typically the postal code for the state of jurisdiction for this case.

  6. Enter the Shift Number for the shift when the employee incurred the injury, if appropriate.

  7. Enter the employee’s Length of Service at the time of the claim.

  8. From the Regular Job drop-down, select Yes if this employee was performing their usual duties or No if the user was performing another job.

  9. Select the Status that best describes the current status of the claim.

  10. Enter the Status Date when the status went into effect.

  11. Complete the information in the panels below.

  12. When you are satisfied with the case setup, click Save.

Contacts

Use the contacts panel to add insurance and attorney contact and telephone numbers to a workers’ compensation case.

Field Description
Insurance Company The insurance company liable for this workers’ compensation benefit claim.
Insurance Contact The contact at the insurance company.
Insurance Attorney The attorney who is handling the case for the insurance company.
Employee Attorney The attorney who is representing the employee in this case.
Telephone Corresponding telephone numbers.

Reserved/Paid-to-Date Information

Use the Reserved/Paid-to-Date Information panel to add compensation, medical, and other expense amounts to the workers’ compensation case.

Field Description
Weekly Compensation Weekly workers’ compensation rate.
Compensation Reserve Amount reserved by the insurance company to cover the cost of compensation in this case.
Medical Reserve Amount set by the insurance company to handle medical claims for this case.
Expense Reserve Amount set by the insurance company to cover the cost of expenses related to the case.
Company Paid Compensation Compensation paid by the company to the employee.
Company Paid Medical Medical expenses paid by the company.
Company Paid Expense Other case-related expenses paid by the company.
Insurance Paid Compensation Compensation paid by the insurance company to the employee.
Insurance Paid Medical Medical expenses paid by the insurance company.
Insurance Paid Expense Other case-related expenses paid by the insurance company.

Related Topic

Adding Workers’ Compensation Case Notes

Adding Workers’ Compensation Case Notes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Action/Workers_Compensation_Notes.htm Path: PD/HR/Action/Workers_Compensation_Notes.htm

Adding Workers’ Compensation Case Notes

Use the Notes form to maintain any notations or reminders associated with a workers’ compensation case that your organization wants to track.

To access this form, select the Actions menu in Workers’ Compensation Cases.

[Image: The Notes form.]

  • To view or edit an existing note, click Note ID.

  • To create a note, click Add.

Either of these actions opens the note entry form, below.

[Image: The Note Details form.]

  1. Enter or edit the Note Details.

  2. Click Save.

Department History Adjustment

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/2019/Department_History_Adjustment.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/2019/Department_History_Adjustment.htm

Department History Adjustment

Use the Department History Adjustment form to change the effective date of a department change for an employee, or delete the department change entirely.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Employee by either typing the name or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records, or by clicking the field label to open the search window.

  2. Click the Date Effective to change or delete. This is the date the department went into effect for the employee.

  3. To remove the department change, click Delete Date. The department change is removed from the list. This is only available if there is more than one row. Click Yes to delete this date.

  4. To modify the effective date of the department change, click Change Date. The New Effective Date field displays.

a. Enter the New Effective Date.
b. Click Submit. The list updates with the new effective date for the department.
— —

Reprinting Form 1099 for Employees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/2019/Reprint_1099M.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/2019/Reprint_1099M.htm

Reprinting Form 1099 for Employees

Use the Reprint Form 1099 form to print an employee’s Form 1099.

Note: You cannot use this form to print Form 1099-R.

Ensure that you are on the correct employee’s Employee Details form. When you open this form, the employee’s ID and name display in the Employee ID field.

To reprint an employee’s Form 1099:

  1. Select the Tax Year of the form you want to reprint.

  2. Select Run. The system downloads the PDF according to your browser settings.

Importing 401(k) Adjustment Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/2020/Importing-401(k)-Adjustment-Info.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/2020/Importing-401(k)-Adjustment-Info.htm

Importing 401(k) Adjustment Information

Use the Retirement Adjustments Import process to import 401(k) adjustment information and to generate an error report and a transaction report. The 401(k) adjustment information is imported using a comma-delimited, tab-delimited, or pipe-delimited text file that is created in Excel.

Note: Note: This form does not use Client Access Group security.

Before you begin:

  1. In Excel, create a comma-delimited, tab-delimited, or pipe-delimited text file that contains at least the following adjustment information in the order shown:

  • Column 1 - Client ID

  • Column 2 - Employee

  • Column 3 - Employer ID

  • Column 4 - Plan

  • Column 5 - Adjustment Date (MM/DD/YYYY)

  • Column 6 - Annotation (Optional)

  • Column 7 - Retirement Earnings (Optional)

  • Column 8 - Employee Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 9 - Catch-up Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 10 - Post Tax Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 11 - ROTH Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 12 - ROTH Catch-up Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 13 - Employer Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 14 - Non-Elect Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 15 - Safe Harbor Type 1 Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 16 - Safe Harbor Type 2 Contribution (Optional)

  • Column 17 - Successor Retirement Deferral (Optional)

  • Column 18 - Successor Catch-up Deferral (Optional)

  • Column 19 - Successor ROTH Deferral (Optional)

  • Column 20 - Successor ROTH Catch-up Deferral (Optional)

  • Column 21 - Loan (Optional)

  • Column 22 - Loan Payment (Optional)

  1. Save the Excel file as a comma-delimited, tab-delimited, or pipe-delimited text file.

  2. Record the file name and where it was saved.

Note: You can use the sample downloadable header file instead of creating your own Excel file.

To import 401(k) adjustment information:

  1. Click [Image: screenshot] (Back Office).

  2. From Operations|Import Processing, select Retirement Adjustments Import. The Retirement Adjustments Import page opens.

  3. Fill in the form. See Selecting Data for instructions about how to select data using linked fields.

Option — Description

Upload File — Click the link, click Choose File, select the file you created, and then click submit. When you are notified that the file was uploaded, click Close.

Delimiter — Select Auto, Comma, Tab, or Pipe. If you select Auto, the system selects the delimiter based on the file content.

Qualifier — Select None.

First Lines to Skip — Select the number of lines to skip at the beginning of the file. For example, the first three lines may provide instructions or overview information.

Download Sample Header File — Click the link to download an example of the Excel data file.

Preview — Displays information about each client to be included in the import.

  1. Click Import. A progress box displays the status.

  2. When the progress box shows that the process is complete, click Close to return to the Retirement Adjustments Import page or Report to open the Job Control Status Report. See See Viewing the Job Control Status Report

========================================================================

Importing Time Off Adjustment Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/2020/PTO_Register_Adjustment_Import.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/2020/PTO_Register_Adjustment_Import.htm

Importing Time Off Adjustment Information

You can use the Time Off Register Adjustment Import process to import time off adjustment information and to generate an error report and a transaction report. The time off adjustment information is imported using a comma-delimited, tab-delimited, or pipe-delimited text file that is created in Excel.

Note: You can access the related report, Employee Time Off Detail Report. This report provides information about employees’ time off. (See Employee Time Off Detail Report.)

Before you begin:

  1. In Excel, create a comma-delimited, tab-delimited, or pipe-delimited text file that contains at least the following adjustment information in the order shown:

  • Column 1 - Client ID.

  • Column 2 - Employee ID.

  • Column 3 - Adjustment Type: T-Taken, A-Accrued, and C-Carryover.

  • Column 4 - Accrual Schedule.

  • Column 5 - Time Off Plan. (Note: Only required if Employee should be enrolled in plan.)

  • Column 6 - Time Off Pay Code. (Note: Only required if Adjustment Type ‘T’ is selected.)

  • Column 7 - Effective Date (MM/DD/YYYY).

  • Column 8 - Adjustment Hours.

  • Column 9 - Accrued Through (Optional).

  • Column 10 - Comment/Annotation (Optional). (Note: If you do not provide a comment, the default comment ‘Imported Adjustment’ displays in the Annotation column in the Employee Time Off Register form.)

  1. Save the Excel file as a comma-separated values (CSV) file or plain text file.

  2. Record the file name and where it was saved.

To import time off adjustment information, do the following:

  1. Click [Image: screenshot] (Back Office).

  2. From Operations|Import Processing, select Time Off Register Adjustment Import. The Time Off Register Adjustment Import form opens.

  3. Complete the form. See Selecting Data for instructions about how to select data using linked fields.

Option — Description

Upload File — Click the link, click Choose File, select the file you created, and then click submit. When you are notified that the file was uploaded, click Close.

Delimiter — Select Auto, Comma, Tab, or Pipe. If you select Auto, the system selects the delimiter based on the file content.

Qualifier — Select None.

First Lines to Skip — Select the number of lines to skip at the beginning of the file. For example, the first three lines might provide instructions or overview information.

Download Sample Header File — Click the link to download an example of the Excel data file. (A file titled TimeOffAdjustmentImport opens in Excel.)

Preview — Displays information about each client to be included in the import.

  1. Click Import. A progress box displays the status.

Note: Although only a maximum of 100 records display, all records are actually imported.

  1. When the progress box shows that the process is complete, click Close to return to the Time Off Register Adjustment Import form or Report to open the Job Control Status Report. See Viewing the Job Control Status Report.

Auto-Enrolling Employees While Importing

You can use the Time Off Plan column (column 5) in your Excel file to automatically enroll employees while importing their time off adjustments.

Note: If you specify a Time Off plan and that employee is not currently enrolled in a Time Off plan, the system automatically enrolls them during the import process. A Time Off Pay Code is only required if the adjustment is T (Taken).

When using this feature, observe the following guidelines:

Scenario Description
Employee that is not already enrolled in a Time Off Plan - If you have an employee who has a seniority date that is not equal to the hire date and you add a valid Time Off Plan to column E on the import, the system enrolls the employee in the Time Off Plan/Register on the file with a Benefit Start Date that equals the Seniority date, and a Calculated Thru Date equal to the Benefit Start Date and the Year End Date, which defaults to the appropriate date based on the Time Off setup. - If you have an employee that has a seniority date equal to the hire date, and you add a valid Time Off Plan to column E on the import, the system enrolls the employee in the Time Off Plan/Register on the file with a Benefit Start Date that equals the Original Hire Date, and a Calculated Thru Date equal to the Benefit Start Date and the Year End Date, which defaults to the appropriate date based on the Time off setup. - If you enter a Time Off Plan on the file, but it does not match any existing client Time Off Plan codes, the Employee ID displays the following message, “Time Off Plan does not exist for this client. Adjustment and enrollment skipped.” - If an employee is not already enrolled in the Time Off Register/Plan combination on the file and the file contains a Time Off Plan, but the employee is already enrolled in a plan/register with the same Time Off Type/Employee ID, you cannot setup in more than one Time Off Register of the same type. (Adjustment and enrollment are skipped.) - When employees are successfully enrolled along with their adjustments, a message displays regarding the job progress, “Time Off Register Enrollments completed: <number>.” - When employees are not successfully enrolled and the file contains the Time Off Plan field, a message displays regarding the job progress, “Time Off Register Enrollments skipped: <number>.” - The process assumes zero (0) hours for the enrollment in the following situations: Accrued and Taken (adjustment for Carryover), Carryover and Taken (adjustment for Accrued), Carryover and Accrued (adjustment for Taken), and when the employee has multiple adjustment types while any type is not represented on the file.
  • If you have an employee who has a seniority date that is not equal to the hire date and you add a valid Time Off Plan to column E on the import, the system enrolls the employee in the Time Off Plan/Register on the file with a Benefit Start Date that equals the Seniority date, and a Calculated Thru Date equal to the Benefit Start Date and the Year End Date, which defaults to the appropriate date based on the Time Off setup.
  • If you have an employee that has a seniority date equal to the hire date, and you add a valid Time Off Plan to column E on the import, the system enrolls the employee in the Time Off Plan/Register on the file with a Benefit Start Date that equals the Original Hire Date, and a Calculated Thru Date equal to the Benefit Start Date and the Year End Date, which defaults to the appropriate date based on the Time off setup.
  • If you enter a Time Off Plan on the file, but it does not match any existing client Time Off Plan codes, the Employee ID displays the following message, “Time Off Plan does not exist for this client. Adjustment and enrollment skipped.”
  • If an employee is not already enrolled in the Time Off Register/Plan combination on the file and the file contains a Time Off Plan, but the employee is already enrolled in a plan/register with the same Time Off Type/Employee ID, you cannot setup in more than one Time Off Register of the same type. (Adjustment and enrollment are skipped.)
  • When employees are successfully enrolled along with their adjustments, a message displays regarding the job progress, “Time Off Register Enrollments completed: <number>.”
  • When employees are not successfully enrolled and the file contains the Time Off Plan field, a message displays regarding the job progress, “Time Off Register Enrollments skipped: <number>.”
  • The process assumes zero (0) hours for the enrollment in the following situations: Accrued and Taken (adjustment for Carryover), Carryover and Taken (adjustment for Accrued), Carryover and Accrued (adjustment for Taken), and when the employee has multiple adjustment types while any type is not represented on the file. | Employee that is already enrolled in a Time Off Plan | - When an employee in the adjustment import includes a Time Off Plan in Column E and the employee is already enrolled in that Time Off Plan/Register combination, the import process performs as normal and makes the adjustments while completely ignoring the enrollment. - The system does not display a message regarding enrollments when a Time Off Plan is included in column E and the employee is already enrolled in the same Time Off Plan/Register combination. |
  • When an employee in the adjustment import includes a Time Off Plan in Column E and the employee is already enrolled in that Time Off Plan/Register combination, the import process performs as normal and makes the adjustments while completely ignoring the enrollment.
  • The system does not display a message regarding enrollments when a Time Off Plan is included in column E and the employee is already enrolled in the same Time Off Plan/Register combination. | Employee that is already enrolled in a different Time off Register that has the same Time Off type | - If an employee is not already enrolled in the Time Off Register/Plan combination in the file and the file contains a Time Off Plan, but that employee is already enrolled in a plan/register with the same Time Off Type, the following message displays, “Employee ID - An employee may not be set up in more than one Time Off Register of the same class. Adjustment and enrollment skipped.” - A job progress window displays this employee scenario failure in the number count of Time Off Register Enrollments and Adjustments skipped. |
  • If an employee is not already enrolled in the Time Off Register/Plan combination in the file and the file contains a Time Off Plan, but that employee is already enrolled in a plan/register with the same Time Off Type, the following message displays, “Employee ID - An employee may not be set up in more than one Time Off Register of the same class. Adjustment and enrollment skipped.”

  • A job progress window displays this employee scenario failure in the number count of Time Off Register Enrollments and Adjustments skipped.

Setting Approval Policies

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Approval_Policies.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Approval_Policies.htm

Setting Approval Policies

Even if you have global approval policies, you can set approval policies at the client level. Using the Approval Policies form, you can maintain policies for several approval types.

Note: Note: You can click View All Policies in this form to view the policies defined for your organization and select one to edit.

Available Approval Types

The following approval types are available:

  • Direct Deposit Change Approval Policy: This approval type uses the Direct Deposit Change option to require approval at the client or global level to store direct deposit information in the employee details record. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Important! There must be a client-level or global-level approval policy in place to use this feature. (Without an approval policy, the system immediately records any changes and applies them to the employee record.) In addition, the approval policy only applies when an employee changes, adds, or removes the Transit Number or Account Number in the direct deposit information.

When an approval policy is defined for a client using the Direct Deposit Change option and an employee, worksite manager, or worksite trusted advisor submits a new or updated direct deposit or deletes a direct deposit, the system sets the update to Pending status and an approval is required. (After approval, the direct deposit follows the regular prenote process, if one is in place.) However, if a service provider makes an update, the change does not go through an approval process and is immediately applied to the employee record.

Note: The approval is submitted either from an employee making an update in Employee Portal or from a worksite manager or worksite trusted advisor making an update in Employee Portal or in the Prism Core application.

When an approval is pending and a worksite manager or worksite trusted advisor tries to make a change, the following occurs on the Deposit tab in the Employee Details form:

  • “Approval Pending” displays next to the Direct Deposit Information panel header.

  • If they select Save or Save & Close a message displays, “A prior direct deposit change is waiting for approval. Your updates will discard the previous changes. Do you wish to continue?” Click Yes to discard the previous update.

  • Late Payroll Posting Approval Policy: This approval type stops payrolls from being posted on or after the Late Payroll Posting date and time during the finalization process, which prevents the payroll from finalizing until it is approved by a user in accordance with the applicable Approval Policy configuration.

Note: To stop a payroll from finalizing, the client must have ACH billing configured on the Billing tab on the Client Details form by selecting one of the following statuses in the ACH Status field, Active, ACH by Division, or ACH by Location. In addition, the approval observes the positive or negative value in the Adjust to ACH Bill Day field, which adjusts the processing of the ACH if it is different from the employee pay date.

  • ACH Maximum Approval Policy: This approval type uses the ACH Maximum option, which you can configure using the Approval Policies form for a service provider. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: The ACH Maximum option is only available for service providers.

You can use the ACH Maximum option to create or update an ACH Maximum Approval policy at the client or global level.

Note: Service providers who have Inquiry access to the Approval Policies and Global Approval Policies forms can view existing ACH Maximum Approval policies in read-only format and cannot make updates to the policy setup.

  • Approval Policy: This approval type uses the Payroll Calculation and Payroll Finalization options, which you can configure using the Approval Policies form for a specific user, such as an HR Role (worksite manager or worksite trusted advisor) or AM Role (service provider). For example:

[Image: screenshot]

[Image: screenshot]

Note: If you do not assign a PTO approver to an employee, all worksite managers/worksite trusted advisors with the H/R Action Approver H/R Role will receive the leave request approval.

  • Client Detail and User HR Role (Standard): This approval type uses the Payroll Calculation option, which you can configure as follows:

  • Control tab on the Client Details form: Select Standard in the Worksite Payroll Approval field for a worksite manager or worksite trusted advisor. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  • Human Resource Roles panel on the Users form: Set up a Payroll Approver role for a worksite manager or worksite trusted advisor. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: These approvals are not processed using regular approval policies.

  • Client Control and Approval Policy (multistep): This approval type uses the Payroll Calculation option, which you can configure as follows:

  • Control tab on the Client Details form: Select Multistep in the Worksite Payroll Approval field. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  • The following message displays:

[Image: screenshot]

  • After clicking OK and then Save, the following message displays:

[Image: screenshot]

  • After clicking Yes, the Approval Policies form displays. Configure a Payroll Approver for the Payroll Approval (Multistep) approval type. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: For more information, see the Processing Multistep Payroll Approval task sheet in the Customer Resource Center.

Each approval policy specifies who must approve an action before it is finalized. There are different levels of approvals:

  • Client worksite personnel can approve actions (for example, a pay rate change entered for an employee by a departmental manager).

  • The internal staff of the service provider can approve actions.

  • Approval policies can also require approval from both worksite and service provider users.

Note: Note: When service providers enter HR-related changes, those changes go through without requiring approvals. Only payroll-related processes made by service providers are subject to approval policies.

Setting Up Approval Policies

To set approval policies at the client level:

  1. Go to the Approval Policies form on the HR|Change menu.

  2. Select the Approval Type, which is the type of action that requires approval.

  3. For each step, set one of the following:

  • Enter the PrismHR User ID, if the step requires that a specific user approve the action.

  • Specify the HR Role for worksite approvers.

  • Specify the A/M Role, which is the service provider account management role.

When you include an A/M Role, you should select the A/M Filter from the drop-down as your organization’s security policy requires:

  • All Users with designated A/M role: Any user with the account management role can see the approvals.

  • Only A/M Assigned to Client: Only service provider users assigned to the client can see the approvals; the system checks the account assignment in the Client Details Account tab for the specified A/M Role and displays the approval for only that user.

Note: Note: PrismHR automatically assigns a number to each approval step you select. The approvals are processed in that order.

  1. Click Save.

Maintaining Employee Dependents

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Dependents.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Dependents.htm

Maintaining Employee Dependents

To add new or edit existing employee dependent information, use the Employee Dependents form. (You can access this form by selecting Dependents in the Actions menu on the Employee Benefits Enrollment form.) If you have selected an employee on the Employee Benefits Enrollment form before opening the Employee Dependents form, the system pre-populates on the Employee Dependents form.

Note: To quickly access the Employee Dependents import template in the Data Import Tool, select Import Employee Dependents. You can use the Employee Dependents import template to import multiple employee dependents for multiple employees at once to reduce the amount of manual work required.

Updating or Creating Employee Dependent

To select an existing dependent record or create a new one, use the Employee Dependent panel.

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  2. To create a new dependent record for the employee, click New. Or, select an existing dependent record from the Dependent ID list. The list shows the dependent ID, name, and type.

Maintaining Dependent Details

To maintain details about the dependent, use the Dependent Details panel.

  1. Complete the following:
Use this option… To define this…
Relation Type Whether they are a Dependent or a Beneficiary Only.
Status The dependent’s status (Active, Inactive, or Pending). > Note: Note: In Benefits Enrollment, only Active dependents show on the Dependents form.
Status Date The date the status went into effect.
Spouse Whether the dependent is the employee’s spouse. A spouse must have a Relationship Type of Dependent.
Last Name, First Name, Middle Initial The employee’s name. The Last Name sets by default.
Birth Date The dependent’s birth date. The system calculates the age automatically.
Gender The dependent’s gender. The drop-down has the following options: - Male - Female - Non-binary: This option displays only if you select the Gender Non Binary checkbox under the Gender Options section of the System Parameters form. - Declined to state: This option displays only if you select the Gender Decline To State checkbox under the Gender Options section of the System Parameters form.
  • Male
  • Female
  • Non-binary: This option displays only if you select the Gender Non Binary checkbox under the Gender Options section of the System Parameters form.
  • Declined to state: This option displays only if you select the Gender Decline To State checkbox under the Gender Options section of the System Parameters form. | Gender Designation | Allows you to define the gender designation of the dependents. This field is setup on the Self Identity Codes form. | | Preferred Pronoun | Allows you to define the preferred pronoun of the dependents. This field is setup on the Self Identity Codes form. | | Social Security Number | The dependent’s Social Security number. When there is a Social Security Number for a dependent and you check the SSN Applied For checkbox, then you will receive a pop-up alert asking you to confirm to clear the dependent Social Security Number. You can mask the Social Security Number based on the option that you choose to mask SSN from the SSN Mask Option field on the System Parameters form. | | SSN Applied For | Whether the dependent requires a Social Security Number. - Unchecked: (Default) A valid social security number is required in the Social Security Number field. - Checked: Social Security Number cannot be entered in the Social Security Number field. |
  • Unchecked: (Default) A valid social security number is required in the Social Security Number field.
  • Checked: Social Security Number cannot be entered in the Social Security Number field. | Tobacco Use | Whether the dependent uses tobacco. > Note: Note: The dependent’s tobacco use affects the life insurance premium calculations. | | Is Disabled | Whether the dependent is disabled. | | Relation | The relationship information based on the dependent type: - For a Dependent, select the dependent’s Relation to the employee. - For Beneficiary Only: - Enter the Relation To Insured. - If this is a trust, select If Beneficiary, Is a Trust. |
  • For a Dependent, select the dependent’s Relation to the employee.
  • For Beneficiary Only:
  • Enter the Relation To Insured.
  • If this is a trust, select If Beneficiary, Is a Trust.
  1. Click Save.

The system validates that required fields are entered and valid (as appropriate). If any required field values are missing or invalid, an error message displays similar to the following:

[Image: Dependent Required Fields Error]

Click OK on the message and complete the required fields before attempting to save again.

Note: The system removes the following field information from employee records when an employee is deidentified (see the Client Administration Guide for details about deactivating employees):

  • Last Name

  • First Name

  • Social Security Number

Tracking Student Details

To track information if the dependent is a full-time student, use the Student Details panel.

  1. Complete the following:
Use this Option… To define this…
Full Time Student Whether the dependent is a full‑time student.
FTS Verified On The date on which someone verified the dependent’s full-time student status.
F/T Student Cert Exp The date when the dependent is no longer eligible for full-time student exceptions.
  1. Click Save.

Maintaining Address Details

To maintain the dependent’s address information, use the Address Details panel.

  1. Complete the following:

Use this option… — To define this…

Copy Address to Other Dependents — Copy the address entered to other dependent records.

Address — The dependent’s address. You can also use the Or Use list to select from a list of addresses. > Note: Note: If an employee enters information in any address field, all other address fields are required except Address Line 2. This applies to new dependent records and existing records you want to edit.

Address Line 2 — Continuation line for a dependent’s address. This is linked to the second Address line on the Dependents form in Benefits Enrollment. Data flows from one form to the other.

ZIP Code — The dependent’s ZIP code and extension. The system automatically displays the dependent’s City and State, which are read-only fields. You can enter it here or click the ZIP Code hyperlink and select it from the Select ZIP Codes selector. On the selector, you can also filter the ZIP Codes based on the State by using the State drop-down options.

Primary Phone — The dependent’s phone number.

Work Phone — The dependent’s work phone number.

Email — The dependent’s email address.

  1. Click Save.

The system validates that required fields are entered and valid (as appropriate). If any required field values are missing or invalid, an error message displays similar to the following:

[Image: Dependent Required Fields Error]

Click OK on the message and complete the required fields before attempting to save again.

Note: The system removes the following field information from employee records when an employee is deidentified (see the Client Administration Guide for details about deactivating employees):

  • Address

  • Address Line 2

  • ZIP Code

  • City

  • State

  • Primary Phone

  • Work Phone

  • Email

Creating COBRA Participant

To indicate whether to enroll the dependent in COBRA, use the Create COBRA Participant panel.

  1. Complete the following:

Use this option… — To define this…

Qualifying event for COBRA — The event that made this dependent a COBRA participant or potential participant.

Qualifying Event Date — The date when the participant qualified for COBRA benefits eligibility. The default is the current date.

Create COBRA Participant — To create the dependent as a COBRA participant.

  1. Click Save.

  2. Click Delete to delete the dependent record.

  3. Click Cancel to clear this form at any time.

  4. Click Close to close this form. Any unsaved changes will be lost.

Downloading the eFlex by Pay Date File

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Downloading-the-eFlex-by-Pay-Date-File.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Downloading-the-eFlex-by-Pay-Date-File.htm

Downloading the eFlex by Pay Date File

This form exports and downloads a file that lists employees in one or more selected Section 125 plans within a specified range of pay dates. Only employees who are available to the user are contained on the exported file.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access this form and download the file:

  1. From [Image: screenshot] (Back Office) > Operations|Action, select Eflex Export by Pay Date.

  2. Complete these fields as required.

Field — Description

Starting Pay Date — Starting date in the range of pay dates.

Ending Pay Date — Ending date in the range of pay dates.

Section 125 Plan — Click the link to limit the report to a selected plan. If left blank, the process runs for all 125 plans.

  1. Click Build. If errors occur, fix them and then rebuild the download.

  2. Click Close to save the file to your local drive.

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee%20Details%20Garnishments.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee Details Garnishments.htm

Viewing all Garnishments for an Employee

Use this form to view all garnishments associated with the selected employee on the Employee Details form. Click the Garnishment Maintenance hyperlink on this form to open the Employee Garnishments form to enter new wage garnishment for any employee.

Note: Note: This new form must be added to the User Roles for user. You cannot find this form using normal search means.

Security

  • Uses Client Access Group security and Data Entity security.

  • Worksite Managers, Worksite Trusted Advisors and Service Providers can access the form.

To view all garnishments for an employee:

  1. Select the employee on the Employee Details form.

  2. Under the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] , click Garnishments to open this form. The form displays the following in a table:

Field Name Description
Docket Number You can click this hyperlink to open this docket in detail on the Employee Garnishments form. You can also click on the Garnishment Maintenance hyperlink to open the Employee Garnishments form.
Category This denotes the type of garnishment. It will be one of the following: - B = Bankruptcy Order - C = Child Support - FTB = Franchise Tax Board - I = IRS Tax Levy - O = Creditor Wage Garnishment - S = State Tax Levy - SL = Student Loan - SP = Spousal Support
  • B = Bankruptcy Order
  • C = Child Support
  • FTB = Franchise Tax Board
  • I = IRS Tax Levy
  • O = Creditor Wage Garnishment
  • S = State Tax Levy
  • SL = Student Loan
  • SP = Spousal Support | Receipt Date | The date when this docket was received from the authorities. | | Start Date | The start date of this garnishment. | | Stop Date | The stop date of this garnishment if applicable. | | Payee | The payee for this garnishment. | | Withholding Amount | The flat amount withheld for the garnishment. The withholding amount will be blank if not a flat amount. |
  1. Click Close to close this form and return to the Employee Details form for the employee.

Performing Employee Fields Import

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee-Fields-Import.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee-Fields-Import.htm

Performing Employee Fields Import

The Employee Fields Import utility imports and updates employee information using a tab-delimited text file or a CSV file into the various tabs and fields of the Employee Details record. There are two sections to this form: Generate Import Spreadsheet and Import Employee Data.

Important! This form enforces Client Access Group (CAG) Security. Therefore, the system skips any employees in the import file who are associated with clients not permitted by the user’s CAG.

To access this form:

  1. Click Back Office [Image: screenshot] .

  2. On the Operations|Action menu, select Employee Fields Import to access the fields import utility. The Employee Fields Import form appears.

This form includes these features:

  • Generate Import Spreadsheet: Create an import spreadsheet for later use by saving existing settings and then downloading the spreadsheet. This aids in performing subsequent field updates for your employees. (See Understanding Employee Fields Import Workflow.)

  • Import Employee Data: Import a tab-delimited text or CSV file with updated field information for employees. (See Importing Employee Data.)

Entering Employee Absences

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Absence.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Absence.htm

Entering Employee Absences

Use the Employee Absence Entry form to enter employee absences from work. This is time off that will affect the employee’s Time Off register.

You can:

  • Enter a paid absence: Sometimes salaried employees’ absences are not paid out on a time sheet, but they need to be subtracted from the Time Off register. When you use an absence code associated with a Time Off register, the system applies the hours as well as any comments you enter.

  • Enter an unpaid absence: Track absences for record-keeping purposes. There are reports that list unpaid absences.

  • Adjust “hours taken”: Adjust the total number of hours taken on the employee’s Time Off register. (To adjust the number of hours accrued or carried over, see Adjusting Accrued or Carryover Hours for Employees.)

Note: Note: Absence Codes must exist before you can use the Employee Absence form. See Creating Time Off Absence Codes for more information.

[Image: screenshot]

To enter employee absences:

  1. Enter the Employee ID by either typing the name or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records, or by clicking the field label to open the search window.

  2. Select the Absence Code for this absence.

  3. Enter the Date Absent, which is the day the employee was absent or the start date of the absence period if the absence spans multiple days.

  4. Enter the total number of Hours Absent.

  5. Enter a Comment for this absence, if applicable. For example, you can use this reference a specific voucher or event.

  6. Click Save. The system generates the Journal ID when you save the absence entry.
