Establishing and Maintaining Employee Address Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Address.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Address.htm

Establishing and Maintaining Employee Address Information

You can establish and maintain employee address information using the Address tab on the Employee Details form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To establish employee address information, do the following:

  1. Go to the Employee Details form on the HR|Change menu.

  2. Enter or select the Employee.

  3. Click the Address tab and complete the following fields:

Field Description
Address Enter the employee’s residential Address.
ZIP Code Enter or select the ZIP Code for the employee’s residential city or town. The employee’s City, State, County, and Geocode populate automatically. You will be presented with a pop-up of Geocode options when an entered zip code spans multiple cities, counties, or localities. Select the correct Geocode to update the address. You can enter the ZIP+4 extension for the residential address, if it was not populated by the system. After you enter or select the ZIP Code, the system checks the street address to make sure that it is valid. If there are typos, missing information, or other unrecognized values, the system displays a dialog suggesting a correction to the address. Click Yes to accept the corrected address, otherwise click No to save as-is or make other edits. Note: If you enter something that the system cannot match to any existing address, it displays the first address in the master list for the ZIP code (such as 1 Apple Drive).
Geocode Enter the Vertex GEO Code for the employee’s city or town to use tax calculations for this employee. After you enter the address and zip code, the system automatically displays the associated geocode in the Geocode field. The employee’s city or town uses this geocode for tax calculations for this employee. If there is more than one associated geocode for that address/zip code, the Geocode Selection list displays. Select the appropriate geocode, which displays back in the form. (Note: If you select the wrong geocode from the list, click Select Geocode to reopen the list.)
Unincorporated Area Select Unincorporated Area if the employee’s city or town is not governed by its own local municipal services and is not subject to local resident taxes.
Portland Metro District Select this option if the employee resides in, or receives pay from the Portland Metro district. This is for highly compensated employees who qualify for the Portland Metro SHS tax.
Home Phone Enter the employee’s Home Phone number. Note: You can initiate a Zoom call to the employee’s home phone number by clicking the Call ( [Image: screenshot] ) icon, if a supported soft phone exists on your computer.
Cell Phone Enter the employee’s Cell Phone number. Note: You can initiate a Zoom call to the employee’s cell phone number by clicking the Call ( [Image: screenshot] ) icon, if a supported soft phone exists on your computer.
Personal Email Enter the employee’s Personal Email address. > Note: Note the following for the Personal Email and Work Email fields: - You cannot enter an invalid character in the email address fields. For example, if you enter a left bracket ([) in the user name segment in one of the email address fields and then move away from that field an error message displays, “Email address cannot contain illegal character: [.” (Note: The system does not remove the invalid character, so you need to make the correction.) - If you try to save an email address that contains an invalid character, the system disables the save function and an error message displays. - If an existing email address contains an invalid character and you do not remove it, when you click Save the system displays a warning message, but still allows you to save the information. For example, if there’s an invalid character in the Personal Email field the following warning displays, “Warning! Personal email address cannot contain illegal character: ‘X’. It should be updated to a valid email address.” Click OK to save the other information in the form (the invalid email address remains in the field).
  • You cannot enter an invalid character in the email address fields. For example, if you enter a left bracket ([) in the user name segment in one of the email address fields and then move away from that field an error message displays, “Email address cannot contain illegal character: [.” (Note: The system does not remove the invalid character, so you need to make the correction.)
  • If you try to save an email address that contains an invalid character, the system disables the save function and an error message displays.
  • If an existing email address contains an invalid character and you do not remove it, when you click Save the system displays a warning message, but still allows you to save the information. For example, if there’s an invalid character in the Personal Email field the following warning displays, “Warning! Personal email address cannot contain illegal character: ‘X’. It should be updated to a valid email address.” Click OK to save the other information in the form (the invalid email address remains in the field). | Work Email | Enter the employee’s Work Email address. | | Primary | Select one Primary e-mail address to use to send email to this employee. | | Deliver Check Home | To deliver the employee’s check to the residential address, select Deliver Check Home. | | Override Geocode | Enter the Override Geocode for this employee; the system uses this instead of the Geocode. (See the Overriding Geocode for Employee Residence article in the Customer Resource Center for more information.) | | Override End Date | Enter the Override End Date when the GeoCode override ends. > Note: Note the following: - Only enter an address in the Mailing Address or Form W-2 Address panels, if the employee’s Mailing Address or Form W-2 Address are different from the Resident Address. (If the mailing address is an international address, enable International Address so you can enter the employee’s Country.) - The ZIP Code field for the Form W-2 address allows users to enter non-US postal codes. Users can also change the City and State fields as needed for Form W-2 mailing. |
  • Only enter an address in the Mailing Address or Form W-2 Address panels, if the employee’s Mailing Address or Form W-2 Address are different from the Resident Address. (If the mailing address is an international address, enable International Address so you can enter the employee’s Country.)
  • The ZIP Code field for the Form W-2 address allows users to enter non-US postal codes. Users can also change the City and State fields as needed for Form W-2 mailing. | International Employee | Select to waive requiring a US-based resident address for an international employee. Important! This feature is only available for service providers. To use this feature, you must enable the system-level INTLEMPLOYEE custom feature code on the System Parameters form. > Note: Note the following: - After clicking Save a warning displays if the employee was paid in the past (that is, the Last Pay Date contains a date), “Warning: Employee records with pay data cannot be saved as International Employees.” - After clicking Save a warning displays if the employee was not paid in the past (that is, the Last Pay Date is blank), “Warning: International Employees cannot be processed in a payroll batch, as they do not have a resident address. Would you like to proceed?” Click Yes to proceed. The record saves without requiring Resident Address information. (If appropriate, select International Address and enter the employee’s international address in the Mailing Address panel.) - After selecting International Employee, data in the Address, ZIP Code, City, State, County, and Geocode fields clear. - International employees cannot process payrolls and should be placed in a placeholder Pay Group to avoid work stoppage issues during payroll. - When the following occurs during payroll processing: - An employee has hours on a calculated payroll - The employee record is then updated to an International Employee - The calculated payroll is finalized without recalculation The system displays a payroll error message and stops payroll finalization, then one of the following scenarios occurs: - If you disable the International Employee field from the employee record and attempt finalizing the payroll again, the system allows the payroll finalization process to complete successfully. - If you remove the International Employee pay from the timesheet, the system allows you to recalculate and refinalize the payroll successfully (assuming there are no other errors). - If you recalculate the payroll with the International Employee field enabled, the system displays a calculation error because there is no resident address for the International Employee and payroll finalization is no longer an available option in the payroll process. |
  • After clicking Save a warning displays if the employee was paid in the past (that is, the Last Pay Date contains a date), “Warning: Employee records with pay data cannot be saved as International Employees.”
  • After clicking Save a warning displays if the employee was not paid in the past (that is, the Last Pay Date is blank), “Warning: International Employees cannot be processed in a payroll batch, as they do not have a resident address. Would you like to proceed?” Click Yes to proceed. The record saves without requiring Resident Address information. (If appropriate, select International Address and enter the employee’s international address in the Mailing Address panel.)
  • After selecting International Employee, data in the Address, ZIP Code, City, State, County, and Geocode fields clear.
  • International employees cannot process payrolls and should be placed in a placeholder Pay Group to avoid work stoppage issues during payroll.
  • When the following occurs during payroll processing:
  • An employee has hours on a calculated payroll
  • The employee record is then updated to an International Employee
  • The calculated payroll is finalized without recalculation
  • If you disable the International Employee field from the employee record and attempt finalizing the payroll again, the system allows the payroll finalization process to complete successfully.
  • If you remove the International Employee pay from the timesheet, the system allows you to recalculate and refinalize the payroll successfully (assuming there are no other errors).
  • If you recalculate the payroll with the International Employee field enabled, the system displays a calculation error because there is no resident address for the International Employee and payroll finalization is no longer an available option in the payroll process.
  1. Click Save.

Note: A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Depositing All or Part of Employee’s Net Payment

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Deposit.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Deposit.htm

Depositing All or Part of Employee’s Net Payment

You can determine whether to deposit all or part of an employee’s net payment directly to one or more accounts using the Deposit tab on the Employee Details form.

Note: Information for the following fields does not display for deidentified employees:

  • Suppress Account Number Print

  • Pay Types to Deposit

  • DD Exclude Amt

Setting Direct Deposit Status

You can set the status for all direct deposits using the Status panel.

To set direct deposit status:

  1. From HR|Change, select Employee Details. The Employee Details form displays.

  2. Enter or select an Employee.

  3. Select the Deposit tab.

  4. Complete the following fields in the Status panel:

Field Description
Deposit Status Select the Deposit Status: - If the status is Active, PrismHR generates an ACH entry for the voucher. - If the status is Inactive, PrismHR produces a pay check during payroll processing for the net amount of the pay voucher. Note: If an employee is terminated and the If ACH, Turn Off ACH? field is enabled on the Employee Termination form, all records for the employee’s direct deposit become inactive.
  • If the status is Active, PrismHR generates an ACH entry for the voucher.
  • If the status is Inactive, PrismHR produces a pay check during payroll processing for the net amount of the pay voucher. | Pay Types to Deposit | Select the Pay Types to Deposit for which the system makes direct deposits: - All: All voucher types - Regular: Only regular checks - Vacation: Only vacation checks - Supplemental: Only supplemental checks - Reg & Vac: Regular and vacation checks - Reg & Sup: Regular and supplemental checks - Sup & Vac: Supplemental and vacation checks |
  • All: All voucher types
  • Regular: Only regular checks
  • Vacation: Only vacation checks
  • Supplemental: Only supplemental checks
  • Reg & Vac: Regular and vacation checks
  • Reg & Sup: Regular and supplemental checks
  • Sup & Vac: Supplemental and vacation checks | Suppress Account Number Print | Select Suppress Account Number Print to prevent the printing of the employee’s bank account number on the direct deposit advice. | ## Maintaining Direct Deposit Account Information

You can maintain the accounts where direct deposits are made using the Direct Deposit Information panel.

  1. Complete the following fields in the Direct Deposit Information panel:
Field Description

Note: The Payroll Deduction type is only available for service providers. - Non-ACH Transmittal: Transmittal not processed by ACH. - Deduction 1, Deduction 2, Deduction 3: Voluntary ACH deduction. You can also enter the Limit amount, which is the maximum deposit amount. - Remaining Balance: Select this method to deposit the employee’s entire net pay amount—or the remaining balance—to this account. If the total pay voucher is split between more than one bank account, use this method for the last account in the list. Whatever amount remains after calculating the previous lines, PrismHR deposits that to the account. > Note: Note the following that occurs when you change the Percentage option to the Remaining Balance option on an existing entry in the following scenarios: - If there is a value in the deposit Amount field, the following message displays when you make the update and click Save: “There cannot be a value in the Amount field on line <line number> when using the Remaining Balance method.” - If there is a value only in the Limit field, the following message displays when you make the update and click Save: “There cannot be a value in the Limit field on line <line number> when using the Remaining Balance method.” - If there is a value in both the deposit Amount field and the Limit field, the following message displays when you make the update and click Save: “There cannot be an amount in either the Amount or Limit field on line <line number> when using the Remaining Balance method.” - Designating one account to receive the remaining balance ensures that the total amount of the voucher will be deposited. For example, the employee splits their vouchers between two accounts, and it is set to apply 50% to each. If the voucher were for an odd amount, the system would generate a check for one penny. If one is set to 50% and the other is set to remaining balance, the half plus the penny would go to the account receiving the remaining balance. - Fixed Skip: Enter a fixed amount that will be skipped if the full amount cannot be posted. - Percentage of Net After Taxes: Enter the percentage Amount of net pay to deposit to the account after tax deductions. |

If the total pay voucher is split between more than one bank account, use this method for the last account in the list. Whatever amount remains after calculating the previous lines, PrismHR deposits that to the account.

  • If there is a value only in the Limit field, the following message displays when you make the update and click Save: “There cannot be a value in the Limit field on line <line number> when using the Remaining Balance method.”
  • If there is a value in both the deposit Amount field and the Limit field, the following message displays when you make the update and click Save: “There cannot be an amount in either the Amount or Limit field on line <line number> when using the Remaining Balance method.”

If one is set to 50% and the other is set to remaining balance, the half plus the penny would go to the account receiving the remaining balance.

Note: If a direct deposit prenotification fails, it remains in Prenotification status. When the client receives notification that the prenotification was rejected, you must manually set the Status to Deposit Inactive (see below). - Deposit Active: Prenotification process is complete. - Deposit Inactive: Deposits are inactive. Typically, you must put new direct deposit accounts through a prenotification process. During that process, the system makes a transaction with a deposit amount equal to zero. Once the prenotification process is complete, set the Status to Deposit Active. The Prenote Date populates with the date of the prenotification. |
  • Prenotification: The system creates a direct deposit transaction for this bank account, with a deposit amount of zero.
  • Deposit Active: Prenotification process is complete.
  • Deposit Inactive: Deposits are inactive. ## Setting Exclusion Amounts

You can set exclusion amounts using the Amounts Excluded from Direct Deposit panel. If the same amount applies to all Direct Deposit exclusions, enter the amounts for each exclusion type.

To set exclusion amounts:

  1. Complete the following fields in the Amounts Excluded from Direct Deposit panel:
Field Description
DD Exclude Voucher Types Select the DD Exclude Voucher Type against which this exclusion amount applies: - Regular: Only regular checks - Vacation: Only vacation checks - Supplemental: Only supplemental checks
  • Regular: Only regular checks
  • Vacation: Only vacation checks
  • Supplemental: Only supplemental checks | DD Exclude Amt | Enter the DD Exclude Amt for this exclusion. This amount goes on a paper check; the system processes any net pay beyond this amount as a direct deposit. | ## Storing Account Information for Pay On-demand Processing

You can store employees’ account information for pay on-demand processing using the Pay On-Demand Account panel.

Note: Note the following:

  • Employees can only have one pay on-demand account where these types of funds will be deposited.

  • You can only use a pay on-demand account when the payroll is marked as Manual in the payroll options. (The system ignores this account for any other payroll type.).

  • The system records changes made to the pay on-demand account information in the audit log.

  • Payrolls set to Pay On-Demand ignore the settings in the Amounts Excluded from Direct Deposit panel.

To store employees’ account information for pay on-demand processing:

  1. Complete the following fields in the Pay On-Demand Account panel.
Field Description
Status Select the Status of the direct deposit pay on-demand account: - Active: Deposits are active. - Inactive: Deposits are inactive.
  • Active: Deposits are active.
  • Inactive: Deposits are inactive. | Transit Number | Enter or select the nine-digit ABA Transit Number of the pay on-demand account’s receiving bank or other financial institution. The Bank Name displays. If the correct transit number is not available, you need to create it; see Bank Routing. | | Account Number | Enter the Account Number associated with the transit number where the pay on-demand funds will be deposited. Note: With the REKEY.DDA custom feature code enabled, if you enter an incorrect account number in this form simply click Cancel to re-enter the account number while retaining the other direct deposit information you already entered. | | Vendor | Select the vendor who processes the pay on-demand funds. Note: When you set the pay on-demand account Status to Active, after you enter a Transit Number and move to the Account Number field, the system automatically displays the Vendor associated with that transit number. For example, if you enter the Pathward (124085244) or the Greendot (124303162) transit number, the system pre-populates the Vendor field with rapid! PayCard. |
  1. Click Save.

Note: Note the following:

  • If you complete some of the fields but not all of them and click Save, the following error message displays, “You must complete the Pay On-Demand Account setup on the direct deposit section.” Click OK to continue.

  • A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Attaching Files to Employee Records

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Documents.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Documents.htm

Attaching Files to Employee Records

You can attach files to an employee record and open any files attached to it using the Employee Documents form. To access the form, open the Actions menu in Employee Details and select Documents.

[Image: screenshot]

To attach files to an employee record, do the following:

  1. Click Load a Document.

  2. In the Upload a File window, click Choose File.

  3. Navigate to the file that you want to upload, and then click Open.

  4. Click Submit in the Upload a File window.

  5. Click Close in the successful upload message.

  6. In the Confirm Document form, the Document Name field populates with file name.

  7. You can enter any Document Notes.

  8. Click Accept. You return to the Employee Documents form.

Once a document is in the Employee Documents list:

  • Click the Document Name to download the document.

  • Click the [Image: screenshot] next to a document to remove it from the company record.

  • Use the ESS check box to indicate whether the document is visible in Employee Self-Service (ESS).

========================================================================

Reviewing Employee Events

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Events.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Events.htm

Reviewing Employee Events

You can review existing and add new qualifying events for the employee using the Employee Events form. These are typically training classes, certifications, and so on.

[Image: screenshot]

To add a new qualifying event, do the following:

  1. Enter the Event Date.

  2. Enter the Event Code. The Event Description displays.

  3. Enter the Action Date, which is the renewal date or date when further action should be taken.

  4. Enter a Comment that applies to the event.

  5. Click Save.

Defining Employee Name and Contact Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Name.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Name.htm

Defining Employee Name and Contact Information

You can define employee name and contact information, as well as related read-only employment information using the Name tab on the Employee Details form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Deidentified employees do not display in the Employee Search Results list when you select Employee. You can only access these employees by manually entering their user ID or name.

To define employee name and contact information:

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window. The employee’s Name & Contact and Employment information display.

Note: The First Name, Last Name, Middle Name, Preferred Name, and Work Email fields do not allow special or invalid characters.

Note: Note the following about the Preferred Name field:

  • Use this field to enter a name that an employee prefers to use, which the system will use in place of their legal first name.

  • The employee’s preferred name displays in place of the first name in the header on all tabs in the Employee Details form. (The first name displays if you do not specify a preferred name.)

  • The system adds updates to an employee’s preferred name in the audit log for tracking purposes.

  • The employee’s preferred name displays in any payrolls that you view in the Process Payroll form and continues to display throughout the payroll process.

  • You can initiate a Zoom call to the employee’s work phone number by clicking the Call ( [Image: screenshot] ) icon in the Work Phone field, if a supported soft phone exists on your computer.

  1. Update the Name & Contact information, if needed.

  2. View the information, such as the EP Username or the various details about their Employment, including their Universal Employee ID, which displays an employee’s qualified employee ID (System ID|organization ID|employee ID) so you can help them register and set up their PrismONE Mobile application.

Note: Note the following:

  • The FLSA Exemption Category field displays next to the FLSA Exempt field when it is enabled and the employee’s position is marked as FLSA-exempt. You can apply an FLSA exemption category code by selecting an option in the FLSA Exemption Category field.

  • The W/C Class is based on the employee’s Remote Location, which is enabled on the Worksite Locations form, and their Remote Position Classification on the Positions form.

Note: Note the following:

  • If the Remote Location field is not enabled on the Worksite Locations form and there is no Remote Position Classification configured on the Positions form, the system uses the standard Position Classification to determine the W/C class.

  • If the Remote Location field is not enabled on the Worksite Locations form but there is a Remote Position Classification configured on the Positions form, the system uses the standard Position Classification to determine the W/C class.

  • If the Remote Location field is enabled on the Worksite Locations form and there is no Remote Position Classification configured on the Positions form, the system uses the standard Position Classification to determine the W/C class.

  • If the Remote Location field is enabled on the Worksite Locations form and there is a Remote Position Classification configured on the Positions form, the system uses the Remote Position Classification to determine the W/C class.

Note: The FLSA Exemption Category field displays next to the FLSA Exempt field when it is enabled and the employee’s position is marked as FLSA-exempt. You can apply an FLSA exemption category code by selecting an option in the FLSA Exemption Category field.

  1. The Status Type Change and Position Change fields are only available to users with correct permissions. Clicking these fields opens the following forms:

  • Status Type Change: Opens the form where you can change the employee status, employee hours, and effective date. Once the form opens, press Tab to populate the status and date. See Performing Status Type Changes.

  • Position Change: Opens the form where you can change the employee position and effective date. Once the form opens, press Tab to populate the position. See Changing an Employee’s Position.

  1. You can include a photo of the user: mouse over the image and then click Upload to add an image, or Delete to remove an existing image.

  2. Click Save. If you changed the employee’s Last Name, the system prompts you to confirm the change.

Note: A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Note: If the selected employee works for another company, the Works for <X> other company(s) link displays. Selecting this link displays other companies where the employee works. (After making a selection, the Employee Details form reopens to that company.)

Tracking Other Employee Information

Tracking Other Employee Information

You can track other employee information using the Other tab on the Employee Details form.

Maintaining Miscellaneous Employee Information

Use the Miscellaneous panel to maintain employee information, including tax- and health-related information for specific states.

To maintain miscellaneous employee information:

  1. Go to the Employee Details form on the HR|Change menu.

  2. Enter or select the Employee.

  3. Click the Other tab and complete the following fields as available and needed in the Miscellaneous panel:

Field Description
Handbook Mailed On Enter or select the date the employee handbook was sent to the employee in the Handbook Mailed On field.
Handbook Received On Enter or select the date the employee confirmed receipt of the handbook in the Handbook Received On field.
Background Test Done Select Background Test Done if someone performed a background check on the employee.
Background Test Date Enter or select the date the background test was completed in the Background Test Date field.
Applicant ID The candidate/applicant number of a new hire who was originally documented as a job candidate and then processed as a new hire by selecting Hire from the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] on the Job Candidates form. Note: If there is a new hire approval, the approval must be successfully completed before the candidate number displays in the Applicant ID field.
Onboarding in Progress Select Onboarding in Progress to indicate whether this employee’s onboarding is in process. To ensure that tax information is complete, the system only includes employees in payrolls after they finish onboarding.
Electronically Onboarded Select Electronically Onboarded if this employee completed the onboarding process through the web.
Electronically Onboarded Date If applicable, the Electronically Onboarded Date displays the date on which the employee completed the onboarding process through the web.
Enrollment Suspended Until Enrollment Suspended Until indicates the date until which an employee’s enrollment is suspended, if the enrollment is suspended see Suspending Benefit Enrollments for more information on related settings).

The Date of the enrollment also displays. You can click the Conformation Statement icon [Image: screenshot] to view the confirmation statement.
Un-Enroll in Electronic Pay Stub If available, select Un-Enroll in Electronic Pay Stub if the employee does not want electronic pay stubs.

This is the default behavior. - Override Work GeoCode with Resident GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s resident GeoCode, defined in the Employee DetailsAddress tab (either GeoCode or Override GeoCode) instead of the worksite location’s GeoCode. Typically, you would select this when employees work from home. See Setting Up Client Benefits.) - Override Work GeoCode with Home Location GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s “home” location assignment to supply the GeoCode for the location-based portion of taxes. This selection is not routinely used. - Override T/S GeoCode with Resident GeoCode: The system uses the employee’s resident GeoCode from the Employee DetailsAddress tab in place of the worksite location’s GeoCode in the time sheet

Tracking Employee Medical Waiver Information for Hawaiian Residents

The Hawaii panel tracks medical waiver information for employees who are residents of that state.

To track employee medical waiver information for Hawaiian residents, do the following:

  1. Enter the Hawaii Medical Waiver Year when the waiver was received.

  2. Select the Hawaii Medical Waiver Reason for filing a medical waiver.

  3. Click Save.

Note: A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Viewing Employee Termination Explanation

The Termination Explanation panel displays the detailed termination explanation entered when the employee was terminated. (See Terminating an Employee.)

Note: Note the following:

  • You can add, update, or delete a Termination Explanation after an employee has been terminated. To do this, you must first enable the EDITTERM custom feature code in the System Parameters form.

  • You can enter a Termination Explanation that exceeds 200 characters when you enable the TERMEXPLAIN custom feature code on the System Parameters form.

Tracking Employee Retirement W2 Status

The Retirement W2 Status panel will tell the system to check the Retirement Plan Box on Form W-2 even if the employee did not have any deductions during the tax year. An example would be for a pension plan where the client contributed if the employee did not. This should only be used if the employee is NOT enrolled in a retirement plan but SHOULD have the box checked on their Form W-2.

To track employee retirement W2 status, do the following:

  1. Enter the Retirement Year the employee is NOT enrolled in a retirement plan.

  2. Select the Retirement Status from one of the following options:

  • Not Eligible

  • Eligible

  1. Click Save.

Tracking Employee Retirement HPE Status

For a Highly Paid Employee (HPE), the Retirement HPE Status panel tracks if the employee met or exceeded the HPE threshold in the prior tax year. For example, for tax year 2024, if the employee met this threshold, then the Retirement Year record would be entered as 2025 with the HPE Prior Year option selected as Yes.

Note: This grid has field level security as it contains salary information. This field therefore will be hidden by default. You will need to enable it using the User Roles form.

  • When enabled, you will have full access to view and modify the record.

  • When disabled, you will have view only access to the form.

  • When hidden, you will not see the field at all.

To track employee retirement HPE status, do the following:

  1. Enter the Retirement Year.

  2. Select the HPE Prior Year from the drop-down.

  3. Click Save.

Establishing and Maintaining Employee Pay Information

You can establish and maintain employee pay information using the Pay tab on the Employee Details form.

Tracking Employee Compensation Information

The Compensation panel displays and tracks employee compensation information.

To track employee compensation information, do the following:

  1. Go to the Employee Details form on the HR|Change menu.

  2. Enter or select the Employee.

  3. Click the Pay tab and complete the following fields in the Compensation panel:

Field Description
Pay Group Select the Pay Group to which the employee belongs. You must assign a pay group to every employee. The system uses the employee pay group assignment and data entered in the Pay Date Control Record to determine which employees to pay on a given pay date.
Pay Period All employees in a pay group have the same Pay Period. This field is auto-populated based on the Pay Group selected above. If on a given pay date you need to assign an employee to a pay period other than the one assigned to the pay group, you can create a Temporary Tax Override Record for that employee.
Pay Periods Per Week This field appears only when the employee’s pay period is “Daily”. - Values can be entered as a whole number from 1 to 7 or left blank. This field lets you define how many times per week a daily employee is expected to be paid (1–7), which supports more accurate daily garnishment minimum net pay calculations when employees are paid fewer than five times per week. - Changing from a daily to a non-daily pay period requires this field to be cleared; you’ll be prompted to remove the value before completing the change. - Updating this field affects garnishment minimum net pay calculations on a go‑forward basis; existing garnishment amounts are not automatically recalculated.
  • Values can be entered as a whole number from 1 to 7 or left blank. This field lets you define how many times per week a daily employee is expected to be paid (1–7), which supports more accurate daily garnishment minimum net pay calculations when employees are paid fewer than five times per week.
  • Changing from a daily to a non-daily pay period requires this field to be cleared; you’ll be prompted to remove the value before completing the change.
  • Updating this field affects garnishment minimum net pay calculations on a go‑forward basis; existing garnishment amounts are not automatically recalculated. | Pay Method | Select the Pay Method by which the employee is paid, either Hourly, Salary, Variable Salary, Commission, or Driver. Important! You must enable all pay codes within a payroll as Include in Variable Salary Pay when using Variable Salary, so the system performs the correct calculation. If you need to pay an employee with a mix of variable salary and non-variable salary pay codes, you must pay the non-variable salary pay codes in a different payroll batch. In addition, if you need to pay an employee with a mix of Pay Types for the variable salary pay codes, you must pay each Pay Type in a different payroll batch. > Note: Note the following when the Pay Method is set to Variable Salary: - Variable Salary only displays if the VARSALMETHOD custom feature code is enabled on the System Parameters form. (This option lets you track the hours that salary-exempt employees actually work.) - The system accurately tracks the hours that salary-exempt employees actually work. For example, non-profit companies could use this option to track the work hours of their employees who are paid a standard salaried amount, so they can use these hours and their allocations for grant purposes. - Employees will always be paid their per pay period amount regardless of the number of hours worked. |

Important! This feature is only available to users with correct permissions. | First Pay Period Hours | Enter the First Pay Period Hours to indicate the number of hours the employee was paid in their first pay period. Typically, this applies to salaried employees whose start date falls in the middle of a pay period. | | Benefit Salary | Enter a Benefit Salary amount to use as an override for any benefit calculations based on salary, if needed. | | Change | Click to open the Benefit Salary Maintenance modal form to update the benefit salary of the selected employee. | | Benefits Per Hour | Enter the Benefits Per Hour, which is the cost of benefits per hour amount. Use the Performance panel to track the employee performance review information.

To track employee performance review information, do the following:

Complete the following fields in the Performance panel:

Field — Description

Last Performance Review — Enter the date of the employee’s Last Performance Review.

Last Performance Rating — Enter or select the employee’s Last Performance Rating code. See Establishing Employee Ratings for more information.

Performance Agreement — Select Performance Agreement if this employee is subject to the terms of a performance agreement.

Job Description Title At Last Performance Review — Enter the employee’s Job Description Title At Last Performance Review.

Next Performance Review — Enter the date of the employee’s Next Performance Review.

Next Pay Review — Enter the date of the employee’s Next Pay Review. You can leave this field blank if it is the same as the Next Performance Review date.

Indicating Employee Allocations

The Allocations panel indicates whether an employee works in more than one department. When PrismHR creates a time sheet for this employee, the departments indicated on the Allocations panel display on the time sheet.

To indicate employee allocations, do the following:

Complete the following fields in the Allocations panel:

Field Description
Allocation Template Enter or select the labor Allocation Template to use for this employee.
Allocation Table In the table, enter the allocation lines as needed. If you leave any fields blank, the system uses the values from the Work tab. - Enter or select the Location code. - Enter or select the Division code. - Enter or select the Dept code. - Enter or select the Project code. - Enter or select the Position code. Note: You can stop the system from suppressing labor allocations during payroll calculation when the home position in the employee details is effective, but does not match the timesheet position (outside of the pay period dates), by enabling the system-level payroll/timesheet code custom feature code, ALT_Alloc_Handling. This is useful when an employee’s home position on their employee record does not match the position in the timesheet (for example, when an employee’s position is changed effective today or in the past, but the effective date of the change is outside of the pay period dates). - Enter the Percent allocation.
  • Enter or select the Location code.
  • Enter or select the Division code.
  • Enter or select the Dept code.
  • Enter or select the Project code.
  • Enter or select the Position code.
  • Enter the Percent allocation. ### Importing Labor Allocation Data

You can access the Labor Allocations import template in the Data Import Tool from this form to import labor allocation data by clicking Import

[Image: screenshot]

in the Allocations panel.

The Labor Allocations import template option and its tabs/fields automatically display when you access this template directly from the Allocations panel on the Pay tab in the Employee Details form.

Note: Note: You can also navigate directly to the Data Import Tool by clicking

[Image: screenshot]

(Back Office), then select Data Import Tool on the Operations|Import Processing menu. In the Data Import Tool form, select the Labor Allocations import template option.

Note: Note the following when importing Labor Allocations data:

  • Allocations must be equal to 100 percent. If not, an error message displays, “Total of allocation percentages must equal 100%.”

  • If you enter a labor allocation value that is not part of the client the following error message displays, “<Record> is not on the file LOCATIONS.CODES.”

  • If you import labor allocations for an employee whose allocations already exist, those allocations will be overridden during the import. The following message displays during this situation, “Labor Allocations imported through this process will override any existing records. Updates to existing labor allocations must be done manually.”

  • Users whose user role is set to DENY for the Employee Pay form (accessed by selecting the Pay tab on the Employee Details form) cannot import data using the Labor Allocations import template and subsequently, the Data Import Tool icon will not display on this form.

  • Users whose user role is set to FULL or INQUIRY for the Employee Pay form (accessed by selecting the Pay tab on the Employee Details form), but whose user role is also set to DENY for the Data Import Tool cannot view the Import icon on the Employee Details form.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Employee Pay form (accessed by selecting the Pay tab on the Employee Details form) can view the Labor Allocations import template, but cannot import data.

  • Users whose user role is set to INQUIRY for the Data Import Tool can view the form and import template options, but cannot import data.

  • Users who do not have access to a particular company through client access groups are unable to import data into that company.

Establishing Alternate Pay Rates

Use the Alternate Rates panel to establish alternate pay rates for an employee.

  1. Enter each Alternate Pay Rate amount as required for this employee.

  2. Click Save.

Note: A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Maintaining an Employee’s Personal Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Personal.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Personal.htm

Maintaining an Employee’s Personal Information

You can maintain employee information using the Personal tab on the Employee Details form.

Listing Personal Information

The Personal panel lists the employee’s personal information.

To define the employee’s personal information, complete the following fields in the Personal panel:

Field — Description

Gender — Select the employee’s Gender.

Date of Birth — Enter the employee’s Date of Birth. This field is required.

Marital Status — Select the employee’s Marital Status.

Employee SSN — The system suppresses the Employee SSN for some users. Users with access permissions can click Edit SSN to view the whole Social Security number.

Citizenship — Select the employee’s Citizenship status.

Preferred Language — Select the employee’s Preferred Language.

Ethnic Code — Select the employee’s Ethnic Code.

Listing Veteran and Health Status Information

The Status panel lists the employee’s veteran and health status information.

Note: Note: Veteran qualifications are defined by the Department of Labor VETS‑4212 Report.

To define the employee’s veteran and health status information, complete the following fields in the Status panel:

Field Description
Veteran Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee is a United States veteran.
Vietnam Veteran Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee is a United States veteran of the Vietnam war.
Disabled Veteran Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee is a United States veteran who has a disability.
Service Medal Veteran Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee is a United States veteran who received a service medal.
Newly Separated Veteran Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee is a United States veteran who recently left the armed services.
Other Protected Veteran Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee is a United States veteran who is protected by the Department of Labor VETS‑4212 Report.
Active Duty Badge Veteran Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee is a United States veteran who served on active duty in the U.S. military during a war or in a campaign for which a campaign badge has been authorized under the laws administered by the Department of Defense.
Tobacco User Select Yes or No to define if the employee uses tobacco products. The system uses this information to calculate life insurance premiums.
Disabled Select Yes, No, Not Selected, or Declined to define if the employee has a disability as specified by the Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990. Note that the employee’s disability status is updated during the Employee Onboarding process by making a selection on the Self ID Disability Form field, which resides in the Other tab on the Employee Details form. See Tracking Other Employee Information.
Blind Select Yes or No to define if the employee is legally blind.
Deceased Select this field if the employee is deceased. The system uses this value to populate the appropriate check box on the Form W-2.
Wellness Qualification Status Displays whether the employee qualifies for the wellness benefit plan. - Qualifies - Qualifies for the wellness benefit plan. - Does Not Qualify - Does not qualify for the wellness benefit plan. To enter a future date for the employee to qualify for the wellness benefit plan, click Future Date. The Wellness Status Change form opens. For more information, see Changing an Employee’s Wellness Status.
  • Qualifies - Qualifies for the wellness benefit plan.
  • Does Not Qualify - Does not qualify for the wellness benefit plan. | Assessment Due Date | The date the wellness assessment is due. | | Assessment Completion Date | The date the wellness assessment was completed. | ## Maintaining License and Automobile Information

Use the License panel to maintain the employee’s driver’s license and automobile registration information.

To define the employee’s driver’s license and automobile registration information, complete the following fields in the License panel:

Field Description
Driver’s License Number Enter the employee’s Driver’s License Number.
Driver’s License Class Enter the Driver’s License Class designation of the license.
License Expiration Date Enter the License Expiration Date when the driver’s license expires.
State Issuing License Select the state that issued the driver’s license.
License Plate Number Enter the License Plate Number assigned to the employee’s vehicle.

Listing Emergency Contacts

The Emergency Contact panel lists employee emergency contact information.

To define the employee’s emergency contact information, complete the following fields in the Emergency Contact panel:

Field — Description

Name — Enter the Name of the person to contact in case of an emergency involving the employee.

Type — Select the Type of contact information, then enter the Contact Info. For example, select Phone and then enter the contact’s telephone number.

Emergency Contact Relation — Enter the Emergency Contact Relation, which is the contact’s relationship to the employee.

Contact if arrested/detained? — Indicate whether the employee’s emergency contact will be contacted in case of the employee’s arrest/detainment on the worksite. (The default is –Select–.) Note: The Contact if arrested/detained? field is associated with the requirement for California employers, that as of March 2026, employers must allow employees to indicate whether an emergency contact should be notified if they are arrested or detained on their worksite.

Listing Testing Information

The Testing panel lists various employee testing information.

To define the employee’s testing information, do the following in the Testing panel:

  1. If you track the employee’s physical examinations:

  • Enter the Next Exam scheduled date.

  • Enter the Last Exam date.

  • Enter the Last Exam Result of the employee’s most recent physical examination.

  1. If you track the employee’s audiograms:

  • Enter the Next Audio scheduled date.

  • Enter the Last Audio date.

  • Enter the Last Audio Result of the employee’s most recent audiogram.

  1. If you track the employee’s drug tests:

  • Enter the Next Drug Test scheduled date.

  • Enter the Last Drug Test date.

  • Enter the Last Drug Test Result of the employee’s most recent drug test.

After completing all the fields in the form, click Save.

Note: A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Tracking Employee Property

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Property.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Property.htm

Tracking Employee Property

You can track any client property assigned to the employee using the Property tab on the Employee Details form.

Listing the Employee’s Property

You can list the employee’s property on the Property panel.

To list client property assigned to the employee, do the following:

  1. Enter the Property Code that represents the client property assigned to the employee. The Description displays.

  2. Enter the Date Issued, which is when the employee received the client property.

  3. Enter the Serial No. of the client property.

  4. Enter the estimated value of the client property in the Property Value field.

  5. When the employee returns the property to the client, enter the Date Returned.

  6. Enter any Comments specific to this client property.

Listing the Employee’s Vehicle

You can list the employee’s vehicle property on the Vehicle Information panel.

To list the vehicle assigned to the employee, do the following:

  1. Enter the Make of the employee’s vehicle.

  2. Enter the Model of the employee’s vehicle.

  3. Enter the Year of the employee’s vehicle.

  4. Enter the Date Added, this is the date that the vehicle is starting use for company business.

  5. Enter the Date Removed, this is the date when this vehicle is no longer used for company business.

  6. Enter the Registration Number of the vehicle.

Maintaining the Employee’s License Information

You can maintain the employee’s driver’s license information on the License Information panel.

To maintain the employee’s driver license information, do the following:

  1. Enter the employee’s Driver’s License Number.

  2. Enter the Driver’s License Class designation of the license.

  3. Enter the License Expiration Date when the driver’s license expires.

  4. Enter the State Issuing License, which is the state that issued the driver’s license.

Maintaining Employee’s Auto Insurance Policy Information

You can maintain the employee’s auto insurance policy information on the Auto Insurance Policy panel.

To maintain the employee’s auto insurance policy information, do the following:

  1. Enter the Carrier Name of the employee’s auto insurance.

  2. Enter the Policy Number of the employee’s auto insurance.

  3. Enter the Policy Start Date, the date the auto insurance policy started for that employee.

  4. Enter the Policy Expire Date, the date the auto insurance policy will expire.

  5. Enter the Policyholder Name, this is the person that holds the auto insurance for that employee.

  6. Click Save.

Note: A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Performing an Employee Time Off Register Inquiry

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_PTO_Register.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_PTO_Register.htm

Performing an Employee Time Off Register Inquiry

The Employee Time Off Register form displays paid time off for an employee, including the details of time accrued and taken. You can also delete or archive a Time Off register for an employee using this form.

The Time Off plan determines how much time off an employee will accrue, and the Time Off accrual schedule determines when the employee accrues time off. The employee’s Time Off register is where the system keeps track of the time off. It is a record of “credit” and “debit” transactions, and shows a running balance. Each time time off accrues, it is posted (credited) to the employee’s register; each absence is subtracted (debited) from the register. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To perform an employee Time Off register inquiry:

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  2. Select the time off Accrual Schedule. The drop-down lists only the plans in which the employee is currently enrolled.

Note: If an employee is auto-enrolled in a Time Off plan, “Employee Auto Enrolled” displays.

  1. Enter the Time Off Plan. Existing information for this employee displays in the form.

  • The rest of the top panel summarizes the Time Off plan information.

  • The Paid Hours Absent Details panel displays each absence taken.

  • The Hours Accrued/Carry-over Details panel displays each accrual earned by the employee.

Note: If you change the Time Off Plan during the plan year ending pay period, you may need to make adjustments.

  1. The Benefit Start Date displays the date that was selected when the employee was enrolled. The system uses this date to calculate the employee’s time off for this plan.

Note: If you need to change this date, you can only change it for employees enrolled in time off with an Anniversary Year year-end basis plan in the following instances:

  • When there are no payroll-generated accruals (calculated hours).

  • When there are manually-entered accruals (calculated hours) or any other manual adjustments in the Employee PTO Register Adjustment form, the PTO Register Adjustment Import form, or the Employee Absence form, even if the comment is identical to the format from payroll-generated accruals. For example, “CALCULATED THRU 8-14-21.”

  • When there are no manual adjustments on the employee’s register.

Note: Currently, if the employee is in a register that has a Fiscal Year End Basis, you are allowed to update the Benefit Start Date at any time.

You can correct errors made in the Time Off Plan Setup. (Originally, you had to delete the Time Off register and re-enroll the employees, if they were in an Anniversary Year End Register.)

Note: If you change the Benefit Start Date during the plan year ending pay period, you may need to make manual adjustments.

When you make a change to the Benefit Start Date, the following messages display depending on the situation:

Situation — Message/Action

When there are no payroll-generated accruals — “Updating the Benefit Start Date for this employee in an Anniversary Year End Basis requires the Year End Date to be updated to <Benefit Start Date - 1>. Do you want to continue with this change?”

If you select Yes — The system updates to the date you entered or selected. Also, the Year End Date immediately updates to one day before the new Benefit Start month/day. (Selecting No reverts to the original date.)

If an employee has payroll-generated accruals — “The Benefit Start Date may not be amended after an accrual has been processed through payroll.”

Note: Note the following:

  • The Benefit Start Date update displays in the audit log. However, the Year End Date change displays as Rpt Plan with the data displaying as a Unidata date.

  • When a change to the Benefit Start Date causes an employee to move from one seniority level of accrual to another, the appropriate accrual takes place in the payroll and records to the Time Off register.

  • The existing behavior for an employee in a Fiscal Year End basis Accrual Schedule remains unchanged.

  1. To prevent an employee from receiving any time off accruals for this plan, select the Stop Time Off Accruals field. A confirmation message displays, “Are you sure you want to stop the employee from receiving accrued Time Off hours?” Click Yes to proceed. (Note: This setting does not impact the employee’s Benefit Start Date and Accrued Thru Date and the employee remains enrolled in the plan.)

To restart an employee’s time off accruals for the plan, deselect this field. (Time Off accruals for the employee immediately begin again.)

Note: Note the following when using this setting:

  • If an employee exists in more than one Time Off register, enabling this setting only applies to each register separately.

  • When this setting is enabled, an employee in any plan or accrual schedule immediately stops accruing hours on the payroll.

  • When the time off accruals stop and the employee is paid under conditions that would normally cause an accrual, the employee’s voucher still displays the Paid Time Off Accrued panel on the PTO tab with zero (0) hours accrued.

Note: The Leave of Absence form and the Reactivation form also allow users to stop or resume time off accruals for an employee by selecting or deselecting the Stop Time Off Accruals field. After making that selection or deselection and if there is a leave of absence or reactivation approval policy in place, when the leave of absence or reactivation is approved that setting automatically registers here on the Employee Time Off Register form in the Stop Time Off Accruals field. (See Tracking Employee Leaves of Absence and Reactivating an Employee.)

  1. If you want to perform time off adjustments for the selected employee, select one of the following shortcuts:

  • Add an Employee Absence: Opens the Employee Absence form, where you can enter the employee’s absences from work. This is time off that affects the employee’s Time Off register. (See Entering Employee Absences.)

  • Make Employee Time Off Adjustments: Opens the Employee PTO Register Adjustment form to adjust the employee’s number of accrued hours or carryover hours from a previous year. You can also adjust the accrued through date. (See Adjusting Accrued or Carryover Hours for Employees.)

  • Manage Planned Time Off Requests: Opens the Planned Time Off Requests form, where you can review and make changes to employees’ time off requests. (See Working with Planned Time Off Requests.)

Note: Note the following about these shortcuts:

  • Only the forms you can access will display as shortcut links. For example, if you do not have access to the Employee Absence form, the Add an Employee Absence shortcut will not display. If you have no access or are denied access to both forms, the header and shortcuts do not display.

  • When you select a shortcut, the system automatically populates the selected employee in the form that opens.

  • After you make changes or adjustments in a form and click Save, when you close that form the adjustments immediately display in the Employee Time Off Register if the form is open to the same plan and register for those adjustments.

  1. If you want to terminate a Time Off register for an employee click Archive, which keeps an historical record of that register so you can refer to this information when seeking answers about what was done in the past.

Note: You must select an Employee and Accrual Schedule to enable the Archive button.

When you click Archive a warning displays stating that any pending Time Off request that have not been approved need to be manually removed using the Planned Time Off Requests form. Confirm this warning by clicking OK.

Note: Archiving a Time Off register deletes the register’s Audit Log, so it is no longer available.

Another warning displays stating that archiving a Time Off register terminates the register for the employee, so you cannot makes updates to the enrollment after the register is archived. Click Yes to clear the fields on the form. (Clicking No returns you to the form to make updates.)

Note: After archiving a Time Off register, the plan no longer displays in Employee Portal (EP) and the system no longer makes additional accruals.

The system places the archived register into an Archived folder as a read-only record. You can review the archived register’s historical information, including the Benefit End Date. However, you cannot make any adjustments to the archived register. (At this time, the Save and Delete buttons are disabled.)

When you select the Accrual Schedule field for an archived record, the list displays “Archived” at the end of the accrual schedule. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • The archived record’s numbering is based on the client, not the employee. Therefore, each number is unique.

  • Archived records display at the bottom of the list beneath any active records.

  1. Click Save.

Tracking Employee Skills and Education

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Skills_and_Education_Details_Maintenance.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Skills_and_Education_Details_Maintenance.htm

Tracking Employee Skills and Education

You can track the skills and education the employee has achieved using the Skills & Education tab on the Employee Details form.

To track the skills and education the employee has achieved:

  1. Complete the following:
Field Name Description
Skills
Skill Code Enter the employee’s skill code or search for a skill code or description by clicking the Skill Code hyperlink and selecting a skill code or description from the list. (You can use the “Refine Search” options to quickly search for a skill code or description.)
Skill Description After making your selection above, the skill description displays.
Skill Comment You can fill in any comments that you may like to add related to this skill of the employee.
Competency Level Enter the competency level of the employee for this skill, using a numeric scale of 1-100.
Date Certified Enter the date when this employee most recently received certification in this skill.
Renewal Date Enter the date when this employee must renew certification of this skill.
Education
School Name Enter the name of the school this employee attended.
Years Enter the number of years completed at this school.
Graduated Indicate whether the employee graduated from this school. Select one of the following options: - Yes - Indicates that this employee graduated from this school. - No - Indicates that this employee did not graduate from this school.
  • Yes - Indicates that this employee graduated from this school.
  • No - Indicates that this employee did not graduate from this school. | Year Graduated | Enter the year in which this employee graduated from this school. | | Degree Earned | Enter the name of the degree earned by this from employee from this school. | | Comment | You may fill in any comments related to this school education of the employee. | | WH347 Skill Trade Classification - This information will be used to populate the corresponding information on the new Form WH-347 | | | Journeyworker/Apprentice | Select an option from the following dropdowns: - Blank (default) - Select this option if the employee is neither a Journeyman nor a Registered Apprentice. - Journeyworker - Select this option if the employee is a journeyworker. - Registered Apprentice - Select this option if the employee is a registered apprentice. |
  • Blank (default) - Select this option if the employee is neither a Journeyman nor a Registered Apprentice.

  • Blank (default) - This option will auto-populate if the employee is neither a Journeyworker nor a Registered Apprentice.

  • State Apprenticeship Agency - Select this option if the employee is registered with a State Apprenticeship Agency. | Level of Progression | This field indicates the level of apprenticeship of the registered apprentice.   • Blank (default) - This option will auto-populate if the employee is neither a Journeyworker or a Registered Apprentice
  • Level 2
  • Level 3
  • Level 4
  • Level 5
  • Level 6
  • Level 7 | Comment | You may fill in any comments related to this skill/trade classification of the employee. |
  1. Click Save.

Note: A save banner, “The current record has been saved,” displays on the tab at the top of the form after you make an update and click Save. (The save banner disappears in a few seconds.)

Maintaining Employee Details

Changing Employee Statuses

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Status_Type_History_Adjustment.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Status_Type_History_Adjustment.htm

Changing Employee Statuses

Use the Status Type History Adjustment form to change employee statuses.

WARNING: Adjustments made using this form affect only the status dates. Users must make benefit adjustments manually.

[Image: screenshot]

To make employee status updates:

  1. Select Status History Adjustment on the HR|Change menu.

Note: You can also access the Status Type History Adjustment form by entering one of the following terms in the Search box:

  • Hire Date Adjustment

  • Seniority Date Adjustment

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window. Information displays in the table.

  2. Select a status row, which highlights in yellow.

  3. Click either Change Date or Delete Date:

  • Delete Date: The system prompts you to confirm that you want to delete the date. Click Yes to delete the status.

  • Change Date: Edit the dates as appropriate.

Note: When there are more records than the original hire date, you can update the following dates for any history record:

  • New Effective Date

  • New Last Hire Date

  • New Original Hire

  • New Seniority Date

  • New Employer Start

  1. After clicking Change Date, a warning displays informing you that adjustments made using this form do not result in benefit calculation adjustments. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Future-dated New Employer Start date changes immediately display on the Employee Details form.

  1. Click OK and edit the dates as appropriate.

  2. Click Submit.

Note: Note: When you update the hire date on the Employee Status Type History Adjustment form, the system updates the date in The employee’s first day of employment field on Form I-9 Section 2.

[Image: screenshot]

(Selecting this link opens the Tax Calculation Overrides form, displaying the employee’s income tax overrides.)

Selecting one of these options allows users to continue accessing 2019 W-4 filing statuses (the other options pertain to 2020).

Cumulative Versus Annualized Tax Calculations

The difference between the two calculations can be marked, especially when an employee’s payment amounts are erratic.

Wages Cumulative Method Annualized Method
Week 1 $500.00 $25.82 $25.82
Week 2 500.00 25.81 25.82
Week 3 500.00 25.81 25.82
Week 4 500.00 25.83 25.82
Week 5 3,000.00 400.82 681.01
Subtotal $504.09 $706.83

In the table, a salesperson receives a monthly commission check. If the amount of the commission considerably exceeds the employee’s regular salary payments;

  • The annualized tax rate calculation returns a tax rate based on a higher tax bracket, resulting in a larger tax deduction amount.

  • The cumulative method calculates the tax rate based on a cumulative average of the month’s earnings, returning a tax deduction amount that is a more accurate representation of the employee’s overall earnings.

Establishing Employee’s State Tax Information

The State Tax Filings panel establishes employee state tax information.

  1. Go to the State Tax Filings panel and complete the following fields:
Field Name Description
State Enter or select the State two-character postal code.
Filing Status Enter or select the employee’s Filing Status for the state.
Allowances Enter the number of withholding Allowances (primary exemptions) for the state.
Sec. Allowances Enter the number of withholding Sec Allowances (secondary exemptions). (Few states require this.)
Alternative Calculation Code Select the Alternative Calculation Code to use to calculate income for the state. This applies only to New Jersey, Arizona, and Arkansas.
Exempt From 1997 until 2021, this field was only used for employees subject to Mississippi taxes. In 2022, this field was extended for use in Colorado tax calculation with no change to the Mississippi functionality. Mississippi: Mississippi employees must provide their state tax exemption claims on a separate form. Enter that exemption amount here. Colorado: Enter the annual withholding allowance claimed by the employee. Indiana: For the state of Indiana, the system can now capture the exemption for “adopted qualifying dependent” on the new hire form. When processing the Form WH-4 (Employee’s Withholding Exemption and County Status Certificate) for the state of Indiana through Onboarding or Employee Portal, the updated Form WH-4 is now available and any entry for adopted qualifying dependents will be reflected in PrismHR. For the state of Indiana, this value will be stored here. This value does not currently factor in this employees tax withholding amount. Support for the tax calculation to include this exemption amount will be included in a future release.
Sup. Exempt This field is only required for employees subject to Mississippi taxes. Enter the supplemental exemption amount claimed by the employee.
Override Type Select the Override Type to use when calculating income taxes for the state, if appropriate. - Add to Withholding: Adds the amount to the calculated value. - Fixed Amount: Uses the amount entered in the Override Amount field as the amount to withhold. - Block Withholding: Blocks the withholding of income tax from the employee’s pay. - Percentage: Specifies a percentage of net taxable pay to withhold.
  • Add to Withholding: Adds the amount to the calculated value.
  • Fixed Amount: Uses the amount entered in the Override Amount field as the amount to withhold.
  • Block Withholding: Blocks the withholding of income tax from the employee’s pay.
  • Percentage: Specifies a percentage of net taxable pay to withhold. | Override Amount | Enter the Override Amount to use in conjunction with the Override Type. | | Multiple Jobs | Select whether the employee has Multiple Jobs and enter their Dependents, Other Income, and Deductions values. Maintaining Work-related Employee Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Employee_Work.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Employee_Work.htm

Maintaining Work-related Employee Information

You can maintain work-related information for the employee by using the Work tab on the Employee Details form.

Maintaining Employee Hiring Status Information

The Status Dates panel lists the employee’s hiring status information.

To maintain employee hiring status information, do the following:

  1. Go to the Employee Details form on the HR|Change menu.

  2. Enter or select the Employee.

  3. Click the Work tab and complete the following fields as available and needed in the Status Dates panel:

Field Description
Last Hire Date The Last Hire Date is the date on which the employee was most recently hired.
Original Hire Date The Original Hire Date is the date on which the employee was originally hired.
Leave Return Date The Leave Return Date is the date on which the employee returned to work from leave.
Employer Start Date The Employer Start Date is the date on which the employee started with your organization.
Hire Report Date The Hire Report Date is the date on which the hire was reported to the state.
Seniority Date The Seniority Date is used to calculate the employee’s benefits. You can adjust it to give the employee more seniority than the last hire date; this can affect benefits, paid time accrual, and so on.
Benefits Thru Date Enter or select the Benefits Thru Date for this employee. This is the date through which benefit contributions have been deducted from this employee’s pay.
Last Day Worked Enter or select the Last Day Worked to indicate the last day on which employee actually worked (if the employee left the client).
Provider Notified On The Provider Notified On field indicates the date on which your organization was notified of the employee’s termination.
Term Reason If appropriate, enter or select the Term Reason, which is the reason the employee was terminated. > Note: Note the following when using this field: - If you select the Term Reason field, the Select Reason form only displays the reason codes set up in the Reason Codes panel on the Control tab on the Clients Details form. - If you enter a termination reason in the field that is not set up in the Reason Codes panel on the Control tab on the Clients Details form an error message displays, “The code entered is not valid for this client.”

If you continue the existing record will be replaced. There is also a future dated change record with an effective date equal to . Do you wish to continue? In the warning message, when you click: - Yes - The warning message closes. All previously entered information displays on the Benefit Group Change form. When you click Save in the Benefit Group Change form, the system replaces the prior benefit group history record with the current data. - No - The warning message closes. All previously entered information displays on the Benefit Group Change form EXCEPT for the Benefit Group Start Date field, which remains blank. (No benefit group history record is written.) > Note: Note the following: - When an Import is used, the Benefit Group is written to the employee’s record using today’s date and the system creates a benefit group history record immediately. - When an API is used, the Benefit Group is written to the employee’s record using today’s date and the system creates a benefit group history record immediately. - When using the Bulk Change utility, the existing functionality does not change but the system creates a benefit group history record immediately. - All New Hire processes (manual entry, Onboarding, Import, API) create a Benefit Group History record using the Original Hire Date as the Effective Date assigned to the benefit history record. - When using the rehire process, the Benefit Group is written to the employee’s record using the current system date as the Effective Date. If you do not change the Benefit Group when using the rehire process, the system updates the Benefit Group to the employee’s record using the current system date as the Effective Date. - Note: Any changes made in the Employment Details panel and the Pay Details panel take effect immediately and do not abide by the date entered in the Rehire Date field in the Rehire panel. (Future dated changes are not supported. If you enter a future date, the system uses the current date.) |
  • If a Benefit Group History record currently exists with an Effective Date equal to the past date entered in the Benefit Group Start Date field, a warning message displays, A change record with an effective date equal to already exists. If you continue the existing record will be replaced. Do you wish to continue? In the warning message, when you click:
  • Yes - The warning message closes. All previously entered information displays on the Benefit Group Change form. When you click Save in the Benefit Group Change form, the system replaces the prior benefit group history record with the current data.

This field is optional. - Enter or select the name of the Form I-9 ID Authority that issued the Form I-9 identification document.
