Maintaining Employee Identification Information

The Miscellaneous panel maintains various employee identification information.

To maintain employee identification information, do the following:

  1. Complete the following fields in the Miscellaneous panel as appropriate:
Field Description
Business Owner/Family Member/Company Officer Select the check boxes as appropriate: - Business Owner: The employee is an owner of the client. If you select this check box, the system treats the employee as an S-Corporation owner, which is not eligible for sheltered benefits on FIT/SIT. (Note: This selection impacts ACA reporting.) Note: If an employee is selected as an S-Corp Principal on the Tax tab (this applies to either a Business Owner or Family Member), all of their benefits automatically default to “N” in the Section 125 field (no check mark) on the Employee Benefits Enrollment form, making their benefits post-tax. In addition, if you mark the employee as an S-Corp Principal after they are already enrolled in benefits you need to manually change the Section 125 plan from Y to N for the employee’s benefits enrollment. - Family Member: The employee is a family member of the business owner. If you select this check box, the system treats the employee as an S-Corporation owner, which is not eligible for sheltered benefits on FIT/SIT. (Note: This selection impacts ACA reporting.) - Company Officer: The employee is a client officer. This selection impacts the unemployment tax in certain states. When you select this field, the system treats the employee as an officer for both W/C and SUTA. This means that the state processing rules set up specifically for officers on the system level are in effect, as indicated on the State Rules form. Note: If you select the Officer SUTA Client Based field, the officer rate will be client-based. You can then set that rate in the Officer SUTA Rate field on the State Tax Bill Rates (SUTA) form (for cost) and in the Officer SUTA Rate field (for billing) on the State Tax Rates (SUTA) form. If you do not set the officer rate, it is employer-based as indicated on the Employer Details form. Note: Some states require Business Owner, Family Member, and Company Officer information for SUTA purposes.
  • Business Owner: The employee is an owner of the client. If you select this check box, the system treats the employee as an S-Corporation owner, which is not eligible for sheltered benefits on FIT/SIT. (Note: This selection impacts ACA reporting.)
  • Family Member: The employee is a family member of the business owner. If you select this check box, the system treats the employee as an S-Corporation owner, which is not eligible for sheltered benefits on FIT/SIT. (Note: This selection impacts ACA reporting.)
  • Company Officer: The employee is a client officer. This selection impacts the unemployment tax in certain states. When you select this field, the system treats the employee as an officer for both W/C and SUTA. This means that the state processing rules set up specifically for officers on the system level are in effect, as indicated on the State Rules form. | Company W/C Officer | Select Company W/C Officer if the employee is the client’s workers’ compensation officer. (Note that this selection can impact the cap on workers’ compensation rates.) On the W/C side, if you select the Company W/C Officer field for an employee or you make an “officer” selection and do not select the Company W/C Officer field, the system automatically determines the employee is an officer for W/C purposes. This means that the state processing rules are followed for the cost side at least (bill side is determined by the bill template for the client, field “Workers Comp High Comp”). | | Tax Credit Eligible | Select Tax Credit Eligible if the employee is eligible for a tax credit under a state-sponsored worker tax credit program. | | 1099 Contractor | Select 1099 Contractor, if the employee is a 1099 contractor. If you select 1099 Contractor, the system will not deduct taxes from the employee or bill them to the employer, including the following: - Year-end Form W-2 - Federal/State income tax withholding - FICA withholding - FUTA and SUTA withholding |

Understanding Employee Fields Import Workflow

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Fields-Import-Workflow.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Fields-Import-Workflow.htm

Understanding Employee Fields Import Workflow

This section describes how Employee Fields Import operates.

The workflow is:

  1. In the Generate Import Spreadsheet section, select the employee fields to be updated. This will assist in selecting employees and will provide greater transparency in identifying the fields that can be edited during the import process. For example, you can select the Federal Filing Status field code to update employees’ Federal filing status in bulk. (This may be necessary at least once a year or you might need to perform this update more frequently.) If you use this field code, note the following:

  • You must select the Include Current Data checkbox to include a column in the spreadsheet that contains the selected employees’ existing data.

  • You must select the Include Filter checkbox to view and use Federal Filing Status as a filter in the Select Employees list. (To do this, select a client in the Select Employees by Client panel and then select Select employees, which opens the Select Employees list where the Federal Filing Status column displays.)

  • If you import “NONE” for the Federal Filing Status, the system updates the employees’ Federal Filing Status field on the Tax tab on the Employee Details form to Select (null).

  • If you import a blank Federal Filing Status and select Keep Existing Employee Data in the Import Employee Data section, the system does not change the employees’ Federal Filing Status and displays the following text in the transaction report, Prior Value Retained.

  • If you import a blank Federal Filing Status and select Remove Existing Employee Data in the Import Employee Data section, the system does not change the employees’ Federal Filing Status and displays the following text in the transaction report, No Values Imported, and the Error Message column displays, Unable to complete row update for Employee ‘:<EMPLOYEE.ID>:’. Enter “NONE” as the federal filing status if you would like to remove the existing status.

You can use the following filters in the Fields to Update panel:

  • Select the Include Current Data checkbox to include a column in the spreadsheet that contains the selected employees’ existing data.

  • Select the Include Filter checkbox to view and use filters in the Select Employees list. (To do this, select a client in the Select Employees by Client panel and then select Select employees, which opens the Select Employees list.)

To delete an existing template:

  1. Select the template you want to delete in the Use template field. The template displays in the Save Selected Fields as a Template (optional) section.

  2. Click Delete Template.

  3. A message displays, “Are you sure you want to delete this template?” Select Yes to remove the template from the list of available templates.

Note: After you delete a template, you can reuse the template name and ID to create a new template.

  1. Once fields are selected, you can select employees using the Employees to Update multi-value grid. (This step is optional.)

  2. Click an Employee ID to use the Select Employees selector. Attributes that were selected on the MVG are used as filters on the multi-selector.

  3. You can now download a spreadsheet in the correct format for entering in new data.

  4. Save the selected fields into a template. This provides faster processing when you need to import subsequent changes. Only the selected fields are saved in the template.

  5. Import the file into the system as a CSV file.

  6. Decide how to handle blank fields in the file. You can either write in blanks in Employee Details, or skip over blank fields and leave in current data for these fields.

  7. Optional: Upload a previous file with updated data instead of downloading a spreadsheet.

  8. After the file is imported, you will receive a transaction report that includes both the errors and the values that were successfully imported.

FMLA Events

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/FMLA_Events.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/FMLA_Events.htm

FMLA Events

Use the FMLA Events form (Figure 70) to maintain the information for an employee’s Family and Medical Leave Act event.

Show me an example of the FMLA Events panel:

Figure 70: FMLA Events

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  2. Select the Event Number to edit an existing event, or select NEW to create a new one. PrismHR automatically generates the FMLA event numbers.

  3. Enter a brief Event Description of this event. The event description should uniquely identify this event from other FMLA events for the same employee.

  4. Enter the date the employee communicated the need for the leave event in the Date Notified field.

  5. Select the Reason Code that best describes the reason for the event.

  6. Select Intermittent/Reduced Schedule Leave if employee will take the leave as an intermittent or reduced schedule rather than full-time leave.

  7. Select Medical Certification Required if this event requires medical certification to verify the health condition of the employee or an immediate family member.

  8. Enter the Date Medical Certification Requested.

  9. Enter the Expiration Date for the event.

  10. Select the FMLA Year Basis type.

  11. If FMLA Years Basis is set to Fiscal, select the Fiscal End Month in which the year ends.

  12. If FMLA Years Basis is set to Fiscal, select the Fiscal End Day of the month in which the year ends.

Hours Adjustment

Use the Hours Adjustment panel (Figure 71) to adjust the total hours for this FMLA Event.

Show me an example of the Hours Adjustment panel:

Figure 71: Hours Adjustment

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Effective Date of the leave.

  2. Enter the Pay Code to use for the leave hours.

  3. Enter the Leave Hours to adjust. Enter a negative amount to decrease the total number of leave hours.

  4. Click Post Adjustment to apply the hours.

  5. Click Yes to confirm that you want to create the adjustment.

  6. Click Save.

Event History

The Event History panel displays the time the employee has already used for this FMLA event.

Show me an example of the Event History panel:

Figure 72: Event History

[Image: screenshot]

Generating and Downloading an Import Spreadsheet

Generating and Downloading an Import Spreadsheet

You can create an import spreadsheet for later use by saving existing settings and then downloading the spreadsheet. This aids in performing subsequent field updates for your employees.

Field imports have an updated format. (See Fields Import Format Changes for details on these changes.)

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

  1. Click + to expand this panel, which is closed by default.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Add field or employee information that you want included in the spreadsheet.
Option Description

To use the field selector, click Field Code (see Multi-Selectors and Dynamic Filtering), then do the following: - Enter a description or field name. Field names now match field descriptions, and the import process supports this. For example, Cell Phone = Cell Phone. - Select a field by clicking the field names to add it to Selected Fields. - Click Done when you are finished selecting codes. > Note: Note: Selections made on the Field Selector become filters on the Employee Selector, if the Include Filter box is checked. (See Multi-Selectors and Dynamic Filtering for more information.)

  • Enter a description or field name. Field names now match field descriptions, and the import process supports this. For example, Cell Phone = Cell Phone.
  • Select a field by clicking the field names to add it to Selected Fields.

  • Some fields cannot be filtered.
  • If you leave this field unchecked, the filter does not display on the employee selector.
  1. Download this information in CSV format by clicking Download Spreadsheet. You can use this spreadsheet to update information and then import back into the system. You can save this data as a template. (See Saving the Spreadsheet as a Template for more information.)

Fields Import Format Changes

The PrismHR field import format has changed. Field names are now the same as a field description. For example, is now Position. Field values also include a field description that precedes the field value. As seen here, Promo is preceded by New Position Reason Code. This corresponds to a field on Employee Details. This example compares the old way of specifying fields against the updated way.

[Image: screenshot]

For grouped fields , this new format also can handle changes with multiple records per field.

Importing Employee Data

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Importing-Employee-Data.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Importing-Employee-Data.htm

Importing Employee Data

You can import a tab-delimited text or CSV file with updated field information for employees. This updates fields on the Employee Details form.

To upload a file that updates employee records:

  1. Enter an Import Name. This is a required field; you cannot import a file without this information.

  2. Select whether to keep or remove existing employee data (provided the uploaded file contains blank fields).

Note: Note: If a data attribute in the import file has a null value and Keep Existing Employee Data is selected, then the current attribute in the system is not replaced with a null value.

  1. Click Upload File.

  2. Navigate to a locally stored field import file, then click Submit.

  3. Click Import. An Employee Fields Import screen appears. Processing steps describe the information saved to employee records and include a date and time stamp. You can view this as a report or download in PDF or XLS format by clicking Report.

  4. Click Close.

Job Candidate Status Codes

Job Candidate Status Codes

Use the Job Candidates Status Codes form to maintain the statuses used in the Job Candidates form; see Maintaining Job Candidates Information.

To set up a job candidate status code:

  1. Enter the Status Code. If you are creating a new status, use something brief but descriptive, for example, APL.

  2. Enter the status Description, for example, Applied.

  3. Click Save.

Maintaining Job Candidates Information

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Job_Candidates.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Job_Candidates.htm

Maintaining Job Candidates Information

You can maintain information about candidates for positions at the client using the Job Candidates form.

Establishing Information About a Candidate

The Job Candidates panel establishes information about a candidate.

Note: Before entering or updating a candidate’s last name, first name, middle name, home phone, cell phone, or location code information, you must either enter or select an existing candidate in the Candidate Number field or select a New Candidate. If you try to enter this information without indicating an existing candidate or new candidate the system displays an error message, “Select New Candidate or select an existing candidate to continue.”

In addition, note the following:

  • If you select Hire in the Job Candidates panel in a blank record an error message displays, “Candidate not found.”

  • If you click Save in a blank record an error message displays, “[Last Name] [First Name] [Sort Name] is mandatory.”

To maintain candidate information:

  1. Select HR.

  2. From the HR|Change menu, select Job Candidates.

  3. Complete the following fields in the Job Candidates panel:

Field Description
Candidate Number/New Candidate Click New Candidate to create a new job candidate record, or enter the Candidate Number of an existing candidate. Click New Candidate to create a new job candidate record, or enter or select the Candidate Number of an existing candidate. If you select the Candidate Number field, the Select Candidates form displays, where you can search for a job candidate by candidate number, first name, last name, city, or state. In addition, you can filter the list to only display hired candidates by selecting Yes in the Hired field. These candidates include those who were hired using the Job Candidates form, or by pulling the candidate into the New Hire form where the system enables the Hired field in the Current Status panel when the employee record is created. (These candidates display “Y” in the Hired column in the lookup form.)
Last Name/First Name/Middle Name/Maiden Name/Suffix Name Enter the job candidate’s Last Name and First Name. If known, enter the Middle Name, Maiden Name, and Suffix Name.
Sort Name The system uses the Sort Name to sort a list of job applicants. You can also use it to search for the applicant’s record. The default is the last, first, and middle names you entered, but you can edit it.
Address/City/State Code/ZIP Code Enter the applicant’s mailing Address, City, two-character State Code, and ZIP Code. Enter the ZIP+4 extension, if known. Note: These fields are optional, so if this candidate is hired you do not need to enter the candidate’s address prior to starting the hiring process.
Home Phone/Cell Phone Enter the applicant’s Home Phone and Cell Phone numbers.
Email Enter the applicant’s Email address.
Referral Source Enter the Referral Source for the applicant, if any.
Date of Application Enter the Date of Application.
Date Available Enter the applicant’s earliest Date Available, if necessary.
Type of Work Desired Select the Type of Work Desired: Full-Time, Part-Time, or Temporary.
Pay Rate Requested Enter the Pay Rate Requested.
Crime Select the Crime checkbox, if the applicant has criminal history.
Pay Period Select the Pay Period for the rate, either Yearly or Hourly.
Position(s) Applied For Enter the Position(s) Applied For. If the applicant did not request a specific position, enter ANY.
Employee ID The employee ID of a new hire who was originally documented as a job candidate and then processed as a new hire by selecting Hire from the Actions menu [Image: screenshot] on the Job Candidates form displays after the employee is hired.
Social Security Number Enter the applicant’s Social Security Number or INS number, if appropriate.
Employee Number Enter the identifying Employee Number assigned to this employee.
Birth Date Enter the candidate’s Birth Date, if known.
EEO Ethnic Code Enter the EEO Ethnic Code for the candidate, or select EEO Ethnic Code to choose from a list.
Vietnam Veteran/Disabled Veteran/Disabled (ADA) Select the check boxes as appropriate: - Vietnam Veteran: The candidate is a United States veteran of the Vietnam War. - Disabled Veteran: The candidate is a United States veteran who has a disability. - Disabled (ADA): The candidate has a handicap or disability as specified by the Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990.
  • Vietnam Veteran: The candidate is a United States veteran of the Vietnam War.
  • Disabled Veteran: The candidate is a United States veteran who has a disability.
  • Disabled (ADA): The candidate has a handicap or disability as specified by the Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990. | Gender | Select the applicant’s Gender. | | Location Code | Enter the applicant’s Location Code. Note: Obsolete worksite locations do not display on the Worksite Locations with User Security list when you select this field. Observe the following: - If you try to enter an obsolete location an error message displays, “You cannot enter an obsolete Worksite Location.” - If an obsolete location code already exists in this field, the system prevents you from saving any updates in the Job Candidates form until you replace that location with a non-obsolete location. |
  • If you try to enter an obsolete location an error message displays, “You cannot enter an obsolete Worksite Location.”
  • If an obsolete location code already exists in this field, the system prevents you from saving any updates in the Job Candidates form until you replace that location with a non-obsolete location. | DL Number/DL State | Enter the applicant’s DL Number and DL State code. | | Over 18/Elig USA/Previously Worked For Us | Select the check boxes as appropriate: - Over 18: The candidate is over the age of eighteen years old. - Elig USA: The candidate is eligible for employment in the United States of America. - Previously Worked For Us: The candidate was previously employed by your company |
  • Over 18: The candidate is over the age of eighteen years old.
  • Elig USA: The candidate is eligible for employment in the United States of America.
  • Previously Worked For Us: The candidate was previously employed by your company | Prior Date From | Enter the candidate’s previous employment start date in Prior Date From. | | Prior Date To | Enter the candidate’s last day of employment in Prior Date To. | | Military Branch | Enter the Military Branch the candidate served in. | | Military From | Enter the candidate’s starting year of service in Military From. | | Military To | Enter the candidates last year of service in Military To. | | Military Rank | Enter the candidate’s acquired rank in Military Rank. | ## Indicating the Candidate’s Status

The Current Status panel indicates the candidate’s status for being hired.

  1. Enter the Status code that indicates the candidate’s current status.

  2. Enter a Next Action Date required for this candidate.

  3. Enter the Next Action Summary, which briefly describes what happens next.

  4. Select Hired if the company hired the candidate. (Note: When you hire an employee using this form or the onboarding process, or by pulling the candidate into the New Hire form, the system automatically enables this field after the system creates the employee record.)

  5. Enter the Hire Date.

  6. Enter the Employee Number, if appropriate.

  7. Enter a Reject Reason if the company did not hire the candidate, or click Reject Reason to select from a list.

  8. Enter the Interview Date.

  9. Enter the interviewer in Interviewed By.

Maintaining the Candidate’s Skills

The Skills panel maintains the job skills the candidate possesses.

  1. Enter each Skill Code that most closely matches the candidate’s skills. The Description displays.

Entering Notes About the Candidate

Use the Notes panel to enter notes about the candidate.

  1. Enter any Notes that apply to the job applicant, as required.

Maintaining the Candidate’s Education History

The Candidate Education History panel maintains the candidate’s education background.

  1. Enter the School Name of the institution that the candidate attended.

  2. Enter the type of education in Education Type.

  3. Enter the start Date.

  4. Enter the subject studied in Education Subject.

  5. Enter the number of Years that the candidate attended the institution.

  6. Indicate whether the candidate Graduated.

  7. Enter the Year Graduated, if any.

  8. Enter the Degree Earned, if any.

  9. Enter the address of the institution in Education Address.

  10. Enter the City.

  11. Enter the State.

Maintaining the Candidate’s Affirmative Action Requirements

The Affirmative Action panel maintains the requirements for this job candidate.

  1. Select Meets Education Requirements if this candidate meets the minimum education requirements. Enter any relevant notes in Education Requirement Notes.

  2. Select Meets Training Requirements if this candidate meets the minimum training requirements. Enter any relevant notes in Training Notes.

  3. Select Meets Experience Requirements if this candidate meets the minimum experience requirements. Enter any relevant notes in Experience Notes.

  4. Click Save.

Recording the Candidate’s Prior Address

The Prior Address panel keeps record of the candidate’s prior address, if any.

  1. Enter the candidate’s primary address in Address 1.

  2. Enter the candidate’s secondary address in Address 2, if applicable.

  3. Enter the City.

  4. Enter the State.

  5. Enter the ZIP code.

  6. Enter the address the candidate moved From.

  7. Enter the address the candidate moved To.

Recording the Candidate’s Prior Name

The Prior Name panel keeps record of the candidates prior name, if any.

  1. Enter the candidate’s Last Name.

  2. Enter the candidate’s Middle Name, if any.

  3. Enter the candidate’s First Name.

  4. Enter the candidate’s Suffix.

  5. Click Save.

Hiring the Candidate

To hire the candidate, select Hire in the Job Candidates panel. The New Hire form opens, populated with the job candidate’s information. see Setting Up New Hire Information.

Note: If there are unsaved changes in the Job Candidates form an error message displays, “This candidate record has unsaved changes. Do you want to save the changes and continue Hiring?” Click Yes to save any changes and access the New Hire form. (Clicking No returns you to the Job Candidates form where you can continue updating the candidate’s information.)

In addition, if you enter information in the Job Candidates form, but do not select the New Candidate field or enter an existing candidate from the list that displays after selecting the Candidate Number field an error message displays, “Candidate not found.” Click OK to return to the Job Candidates form.

Setting Up Labor Union Codes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Labor_Union_Codes.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Labor_Union_Codes.htm

Setting Up Labor Union Codes

Labor union codes are maintained by the client company and are used for calculations where union benefits and dues are required. The system can prepare reports based on the union for use in properly remitting amounts due to the various locals involved.

The major steps in processing labor union codes are:

  • Adding a labor union code

  • Applying a labor union code to an employee

  • Defining deduction codes

  • Assigning deduction codes to the employee record

Adding a Labor Union Code

Set up union codes with the Labor Unions form

To add a union code:

  1. Select Client.
  2. From Client|Change, select Labor Unions.
  3. Complete these fields as required. Field — Description Union Code — Enter a new union code, or select an existing code by clicking the Union Code link. Union Name — Enter the name of the union. Union Bill Template — Select a template for employees that belong to this union. For default bundled clients, this changes the billing amounts. Union Deduction Code — Select a deduction code for reporting. A description displays after a code is selected. Custom Option Code — Do not use this field unless directed to do so by the PrismHR Customer Support Team. In that case, enter the custom code provided to you in this field. Rule — Select a rule to calculate union dues. For example, Fixed Amount charges the same amount for each pay period. Rate/Perc — Optional: Enter a rate/percentage to calculate dues if either the Percentage of Wages or Rate x Hours Worked rule was selected. Rate Group — Enter a rate group if the rule line should be grouped with others for performing calculations. Any Cap or Min/Max should point to this grouping code. Rate Ge — Enter the Rate Ge (Rate Greater than or equal to) if this rule applies to rates between two points. Rate Le — Enter the Rate Le (Rate Less than or equal to) if this rule is less than or equal to a certain amount. Rule Start — Enter the rule start date. Leave this blank to put the rule into effect immediately. Rule End — Enter the rule end date, if any. Leave this blank if the rule never expires. Pay Code — Select a fringe benefit pay code. Rate — Enter a rate for this fringe benefit. The value multiplied against this rate is determined by the calculation method defined on the pay code. Start Date — Date when the benefit starts. Stop Date — Date when the benefit ends.
  4. Enter the Union Address information.

Applying a Union Affiliation to an Employee

Assign a Labor Union Affiliation to an employee with the Assignments panel.

To assign a labor union affiliation to this employee:

  1. Click the HR menu.

  2. From HR|Change, select Employee Details.

  3. Enter an employee by typing their name or ID to display a list of matching employee records. Or, click the field label to open a search window.

  4. Select the Work tab, and scroll to the Assignments panel.

  5. Select a labor union affiliation and start date.

Creating a Union Dues Deduction Code

Create union dues deduction codes with the Deduction Codes form.

To create a union dues deduction code:

  1. Click Back Office.

  2. From System|Change select Deduction Codes.

  3. Set these fields:

Field Description
Calculation Method Set the Calculation Method to Union Dues Table.
Deduction Type Set the Deduction Type to Voluntary (used for most union dues deduction codes).

  1. Enter the rest of the required information for this deduction code. See the Payroll User Guide for more information about creating deduction codes.

  2. Click Save.

Assigning Deduction Codes to an Employee

Assign a union dues deduction code to an employee with the Recurring Deductions form.

To assign a union dues deduction code to an employee:

  1. Select Payroll.

  2. From Payroll|Change, select Recurring Deductions.

  3. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  4. Click + and add the union Deduction Code to the table.

  5. Enter an Amount for the union dues deduction.

  6. Enter the deduction Periods for which this union deduction code will be deducted from the employee’s net earnings.

  7. Click Save.

Multi-Selectors and Dynamic Filtering

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Multi-Selectors%20and%20Dynamic%20Filtering.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Multi-Selectors and Dynamic Filtering.htm

Multi-Selectors and Dynamic Filtering

A multi-selector lets you make multiple selections for fields you want to update for employees. Up to six of the selected fields can be designated as filters to be used in the Employee Selector.

Fields to Update (Select Field Selector)

This selector lets you add field codes for use in an import spreadsheet.

To select fields:

  1. Add fields to the Fields to Update table by clicking the Field Code link in the column header. This launches the Select Field selector. [Image: screenshot]

  2. Enter a field description or name, or select fields from the pick list on top of the selector by double-clicking them. The Records Selected table is populated with your selections. You can remove a selection by clicking the x icon on the right side of the table.

  3. Optional: Filter results further by selecting a Field Set. For example, Labor Union Affiliation.

  4. When you are finished making selections, click Done. You are returned to the Fields to Update table with your selections displayed. [Image: screenshot]

  5. Select fields you want to filter on for employees by clicking Include Filter next to each field. You can only select six fields to use as filters. If you select more than six fields, the system warns you that only 6 filters can be added to the Employee Selector.

Note: Note: Any field data that is changed in Fields Import will appear in the audit log on the Employee Details form.

[Image: screenshot]

Employees to Update (Select Employee Selector)

Use the Select Employee Selector to specify a group of employees to include in a spreadsheet you can download for later use. Up to six attributes can be specified on the Select Field Selector for use as dynamic filters on Select Employee. For example, Pay Method and Location can be pre-selected as filters. This allows you to narrow down your search for specific employees who have these attributes applied to them.

To launch this selector and make employee selections:

  1. Click Employee ID in the Employees to Update table. This opens the Select Employees Selector.

  2. Enter Employee ID or Employee Name, or select employees from the pick list on top of the selector by double-clicking them. The Selected Employees table is populated with your selections.

  3. Filter results further by selecting one of the dynamic filters you previously specified. For example, Pay Method.

  4. When you are finished making selections, click Done. You are returned to the Employees to Update multi-value grid with your selections displayed . This specifies the employees that will appear on the spreadsheet you will download. [Image: screenshot] [Image: screenshot]

  5. Download your settings as a spreadsheet. (See Generating and Downloading an Import Spreadsheet.)

  6. Save your settings as a template. (See Saving the Spreadsheet as a Template.)

Creating and Updating OSHA Case Files

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/OSHA_Cases.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/OSHA_Cases.htm

Creating and Updating OSHA Case Files

Use the OSHA Cases form to create and update Occupational Safety & Health Administration (OSHA) case files for work-related injuries or illnesses.

OSHA Case

The first panel establishes basic information for the OSHA case file.

To set up or modify an OSHA case:

  1. Click New Case to create a new OSHA case file, or enter the Case Number of an existing case file.

  2. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  3. If this case involves privacy concerns, select the Privacy Case? option. If this field is selected, the OSHA 300 Log will display the employee name as “Privacy Case”.

  4. Enter the employee’s Position. The default comes from the Employee Details; do not enter the activity in which the employee was engaged.

  5. Enter the code for home Department code in which the employee worked when the injury occurred or at the onset of the illness. The default comes from the Employee Details.

  6. Enter the Location code where the injury or illness occurred. The description displays.

  7. Modify the fields in the panels listed below, as necessary.

  8. Click Save.

Injury/Illness

Use the Injury/Illness panel to describe what happened in the OSHA case.

Field Description
Date of Injury or Onset of Illness Date when the employee sustained the injury or first reported the symptoms.
OSHA Event Code for Injury/Illness Enter the event code that best describes the injury or illness.
Report Incident on the 300A log Select this option if the client recorded the injury or illness in the OSHA 300A log.
Description of Injury/Illness Enter a description. Be as explicit as possible.
Description of where accident occurred This is an optional field and is limited to 15 characters.

Claim

The Claim panel provides additional OSHA claim information.

Field Description
Date Notified Date when the employee notified their supervisor about the injury or illness.
Fatal Select one of the following options: - Unknown - to indicate that the condition of the employee is not known. - No - to indicate that the injury or illness was not fatal. - Yes - to indicate that the injury or illness was fatal.
  • Unknown - to indicate that the condition of the employee is not known.
  • No - to indicate that the injury or illness was not fatal.
  • Yes - to indicate that the injury or illness was fatal. | Expired On | If you select Yes above, enter the date of the expiration of the claim to ensure reporting on the OSHA 300A Report. | | Claim Filed | Select one of the following options: - Unknown - to indicate that the claim filing status of the employee is not known. - No - to indicate that the claim has not been filed. - Yes - to indicate that the claim has been filed. |
  • Unknown - to indicate that the claim filing status of the employee is not known.
  • No - to indicate that the claim has not been filed.
  • Yes - to indicate that the claim has been filed. | Days Away | Number of days away from work as a result of the injury or illness. | | Days Restricted | Number of days the employee has restricted work duties. | | Medical Cost | Total estimated medical cost associated with the illness or injury. Enter 0 if there is no estimate or if it does not apply to this case. | | Labor Cost | Enter the total labor cost due to this illness or injury. Enter 0 if there is no estimate or if it does not apply to this case. | ## OSHA Form 101 Detail

Enter detailed information as required in the OSHA Form 101 Detail panel.

[Image: screenshot]

To fill out information for OSHA Form 101:

  1. Enter the Body Part Description. This describes what was affected by the injury or illness.

  2. Describe the task in which the employee was engaged.

  3. Describe in detail the events that resulted in the injury or illness.

  4. Name the object or substance that affected the employee. For example:

  • The machine or other object that struck the employee, or which he or she struck

  • The vapor or poison the employee inhaled or swallowed

  • The chemical or radiation that irritated the skin

  • The object that the employee lifted or pulled

OSHA Other Detail

Enter other information in the OSHA Other Detail panel, as required.

[Image: screenshot]

Field Description
Report Date Date when the report was prepared.
Prepared By Person who prepared the report.
Preparer Position Job title of the person who prepared the report.
Physician Name Name of the physician who treated the employee for the injury or illness.
Physician Address Street address for the physician.
City, State, & ZIP code Location of the physician.
Hospital Name Name of the hospital where the employee received treatment for the injury or illness.
Hospital Address Street address of the hospital.
City, State & ZIP Code Hospital location.

Changing an Employee’s User Password

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Password_Change_Form_EE-Details.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Password_Change_Form_EE-Details.htm

Changing an Employee’s User Password

Use the Change Password form to change an employee’s user password. We recommend that you enter a password that you intend to be temporary, and instruct the employee to change it the next time they log in to Employee Portal (EP).

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Verify the Username is the correct user.

  2. Enter the New Password. The system validates the password; if it does not pass the validation, PrismHR informs you what you need to do to create a valid password.

  3. Reenter the password in the Confirm New Password field.

  4. Click Save. The next time the employee logs on, he or she will use the new password.

This resets the lockout period, if any, in cases where you are changing the password because the employee was locked out of EP due to too many failed login attempts.

Changing Effective Date for Employee Pay Rate Changes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Pay_History_Adjustment.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Pay_History_Adjustment.htm

Changing Effective Date for Employee Pay Rate Changes

Use the Pay History Adjustment form to change the effective date of a pay rate change for an employee, or delete the pay rate change entirely.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  2. Click the Date Effective to change or delete the date. This is the date the pay rate went into effect for the employee.

  • To remove the pay rate change, click Delete Date. The pay rate change is removed from the list. (This is only available if there is more than one row.)

Note: A warning message displays indicating that deleting a pay rate change for the current timesheet may not update the current timesheet. Click Yes to continue.

Important! Deleting a pay rate change that is effective on the first day of the current timesheet does not automatically update the pay rate on that timesheet.

  • To modify the effective date of the pay rate change, click Change Date. The New Effective Date field displays.

[Image: screenshot]

a. Enter the New Effective Date.
b. Click Submit. The list updates with the new effective date for the pay rate.
— —

Note: Note: If you modify the effective date multiple times for the same period, a warning displays stating that this action may affect any Retro Pay calculations that have been paid or are due to the employee.

Specifying Time Off Auto-enroll Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/PTO_Auto_Enroll_Rules.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/PTO_Auto_Enroll_Rules.htm

Specifying Time Off Auto-enroll Rules

Specify plans that automatically enroll new employees with the Time Off Auto Enroll Rules form. Companies with existing rules display information similar to this:

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note the following:

  • If there are Time Off Auto Enroll Rules available for the active client and you delete all existing rules, the form refreshes and the Save and Delete buttons no longer display.

  • When an approval is approved for an applicable change, the system re-evaluates Time Off auto-enroll rules and executes enrollments in the Time Off Auto Enroll Rules form for current or past-dated Status/Type and Position changes. If the change date matches the system date or a prior date the following occurs:

  • If an employee meets the criteria for a rule and the rule is set to archive in the prior Time Off Register, the system archives existing Time Off Registers that share the same class as the Time Off Register assigned to the affected rule.

Note: In this scenario a message displays, “A type change has taken place that will archive this employee’s Time Off plan.”

  • If an employee meets the criteria for a rule and the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and no Time Off Type conflicts exist with the employee’s existing register, the system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan.

  • If an employee meets the criteria for a rule and the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and a Time Off Type conflict exists, the system does not update the Time Off Register.

  • If an employee does not meet the criteria for any rule, the system does not update the Time Off Register.

Creating a Time Off Auto-enroll Rule

To create an auto-enroll rule:

  1. Select Client.

  2. From Client|Change, select Time Off Auto Enroll Rules.

  3. Click New Rule to view the Time Off Auto-Enrollment Rules Maintenance form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Select Include (default) or Exclude for each panel and complete the information as required.
Field/Panel Description
If appropriate, add values in the panels: Employee Type, Location, Work Group, Position, Department, Pay Method, Benefit Group ID, Division, and Pay Group. Only employees who have all these conditions are enrolled in the plan. Note: The Variable Salary option only displays in the Pay Method field if the VARSALMETHOD custom feature code is enabled on the System Parameters form.
Enroll them in this type of Time Off Enter or select the qualifying Accrual Schedule and Time Off Plan in which to automatically enroll employees who meet the conditions: - Accrual Schedule: Select the schedule code that determines when employees accrue time off. After making a selection, the description displays. - Time Off Plan: Select the plan code that determines how much time off employees will accrue. After making a selection, the description displays.
  • Accrual Schedule: Select the schedule code that determines when employees accrue time off. After making a selection, the description displays.
  • Time Off Plan: Select the plan code that determines how much time off employees will accrue.   1. Click Save.

Note: Note: Users can enroll employees in the Time Off plans using the Time Off Auto Register Enrollment feature, found in the HR menu under HR|Action.

To apply this setting:

  1. Do the following:
Field Description

  • Hire Date: Displays as H after selecting

Defining Multiple Rule Triggers

You can indicate which events trigger the re-assessment of a rule and whether to automatically archive existing Time Off Registers when the rule is invoked by setting the options listed below.

Note: You cannot use a rule trigger option more than once. If you try to reuse a trigger an error message displays, “Trigger already listed for this rule.” Click OK to continue.

To define multiple triggers for a rule:

  1. Complete the following:
Field Description
Trigger Rule On Select one of the following settings: - Status/Type Change: Rule triggers during an employee status/type change process and, if criteria are met, the system enrolls that employee in the indicated plan. When a Time Off auto-enroll rule is configured with the Status/Type Change setting and an employee status/type change occurs, the following happens: If the change datematches or is priorto the system date and there are no required approvals: - If the rule is set to archive the prior Time Off Register: - Any existing Time Off Registers that share the same class as the Time Off Register assigned to the affecting rule are archived. - The system sends a notification about register archival to the user on the Status Type Change form. - The system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan. - If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and no Time Off Type conflicts exist with the employee’s existing register, the system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan. - If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and a Time Off Type conflict exists, the system does not update the Time Off Register and does not make any changes. The systemdoes notupdate the Time Off Register anddoes notmake any changes in the following scenarios: - If the change date matches or is prior to the system date and there are required approvals. - If the change date is future-dated and there are required approvals. - If the change date is future-dated and there are no required approvals. Note: When multiple Time Off auto-enroll rules for the same Time Off type are set to trigger during the Status/Type Change process, the system evaluates and enforces the rules by Rule Number order. Therefore, if an employee meets the criteria for a rule, the evaluation of all other rules for the same Time Off type stops and the process moves on to evaluate any other Time Off types represented in the Time Off auto-enroll rules. - Position Change: Rule triggers during an employee position change process and, if criteria are met, the system enrolls that employee in the indicated plan. When a Time Off auto-enroll rule is configured with the Position Change setting and an employee position change occurs, the following happens: If the change datematches or is priorto the system date and there are no required approvals: - If the rule is set to archive the prior Time Off Register: - Any existing Time Off Registers that share the same class as the Time Off Register assigned to the affecting rule are archived. - The system sends a notification about register archival to the user on the Position Change form. - The system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan. - If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and no Time Off Type conflicts exist with the employee’s existing register, the system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan. - If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and a Time Off Type conflict exists, the system does not update the Time Off Register and does not make any changes. The systemdoes notupdate the Time Off Register anddoes notmake any changes in the following scenarios: - If the change date matches or is prior to the system date and there are required approvals. - If the change date is future-dated and there are required approvals. - If the change date is future-dated and there are no required approvals. Note: When multiple Time Off auto-enroll rules for the same Time Off type are set to trigger during the Position Change process, the system evaluates and enforces the rules by Rule Number order. Therefore, if an employee meets the criteria for a rule, the evaluation of all other rules for the same Time Off type stops and the process moves on to evaluate any other Time Off types represented in the Time Off auto-enroll rules. > Note: Note the following: - You cannot use a rule trigger option more than once. If you try to reuse a trigger an error message displays, “Trigger already listed for this rule.” Click OK to continue. In addition, you can view existing Time Off Auto-enrollment rules, but you cannot make any updates to them. - Using future-dated status/type changes and position changes triggers a re-evaluation of the Time Off auto-enroll rules and executes the enrollment based on the employee’s Status/Type Change or Position Change at the time the change takes effect.
  • Status/Type Change: Rule triggers during an employee status/type change process and, if criteria are met, the system enrolls that employee in the indicated plan.
  • If the rule is set to archive the prior Time Off Register:
  • Any existing Time Off Registers that share the same class as the Time Off Register assigned to the affecting rule are archived.
  • The system sends a notification about register archival to the user on the Status Type Change form.
  • The system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan.
  • If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and no Time Off Type conflicts exist with the employee’s existing register, the system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan.
  • If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and a Time Off Type conflict exists, the system does not update the Time Off Register and does not make any changes.
  • If the change date matches or is prior to the system date and there are required approvals.
  • If the change date is future-dated and there are required approvals.
  • If the change date is future-dated and there are no required approvals.
  • Position Change: Rule triggers during an employee position change process and, if criteria are met, the system enrolls that employee in the indicated plan.
  • If the rule is set to archive the prior Time Off Register:
  • Any existing Time Off Registers that share the same class as the Time Off Register assigned to the affecting rule are archived.
  • The system sends a notification about register archival to the user on the Position Change form.
  • The system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan.
  • If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and no Time Off Type conflicts exist with the employee’s existing register, the system creates a new enrollment/register for the employee’s new plan.
  • If the rule is not set to archive the prior Time Off Register and a Time Off Type conflict exists, the system does not update the Time Off Register and does not make any changes.
  • If the change date matches or is prior to the system date and there are required approvals.
  • If the change date is future-dated and there are required approvals.
  • If the change date is future-dated and there are no required approvals.
  • You cannot use a rule trigger option more than once. If you try to reuse a trigger an error message displays, “Trigger already listed for this rule.” Click OK to continue. In addition, you can view existing Time Off Auto-enrollment rules, but you cannot make any updates to them.
  • Using future-dated status/type changes and position changes triggers a re-evaluation of the Time Off auto-enroll rules and executes the enrollment based on the employee’s Status/Type Change or Position Change at the time the change takes effect. | Archive existing register? | Select Yes to archive existing Time Off registers when the rule is invoked based on the re-assessment of the rule. Note: You must select an option. Otherwise, an error message displays. Click OK to continue. |

  1. Click Save. The Time Off Auto Enroll Rules form displays.

Importing Time Off Auto-enrollment Rules

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/PTO_Auto_Enrollment_Rules_Import.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/PTO_Auto_Enrollment_Rules_Import.htm

Importing Time Off Auto-enrollment Rules

Use the Time Off Auto-Enrollment Rules Import form to import multiple Time Off auto-enrollment rules or update existing rules. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To access the form, click the Back Office menu. Under Operations|Action, click Time Off Auto-Enrollment Rules Import.

To import Time Off auto-enrollment rules:

  1. In the Generate Import Spreadsheet section, create an import spreadsheet by entering or selecting the Client IDs. (You can select multiple clients from the Select Clients list at once.) The Client Name displays next to each client. (If left blank, the system selects all clients and bill codes.)

  2. Select Download Spreadsheet. The system creates a spreadsheet that displays in a separate window, which is suitable for editing.

  3. In the Import Data section, enter an Import Name. (This is a required field; you cannot import a file without this information.)

  4. Select Upload File. Click Add Files and select the file to upload, then click Start Upload. (This file must be in .csv format.)

  5. Click Close. A separate Downloads window displays the name of the .csv file that the system created. (Click Open file to view the downloaded file.)

Note: The first row in the downloaded file displays information about the first rule and the multi-value fields, which are separated by a pipe delimiter (|).

  1. Click Import. Processing steps describe the information saved and include a date and time stamp. You can view this as a report or download in PDF or XLS format by clicking Report.

  2. Click Close.

  3. Click Transaction Report to display a link to a report that contains the Time Off auto-enrollment rules transactions.

Note: The Transaction Report displays “Skipped” for any column in a row that has valid data but was not imported because a required row contained invalid data. (All invalid data displays in the Transaction Report.)

  1. Click Error Report to display a link to a .csv file that details the errors that occurred during the import. (The system does not import any rows listed in the error file and only imports rows where all data in the row is valid.)

Note: The Error Report link only displays if errors occurred during the import, which confirms that the system is unable to perform the import.

Note: Note the following:

  • You can only select active clients in the Client ID field.

  • If you enter an inactive client or a client that you are not allowed to access the following error message displays, “Invalid Client ID.”

  • You cannot move rules to a new sort order.

  • You can make changes to existing rules.

  • New rules are marked as “NEW” for the Rule Number in the import.

========================================================================

Adjusting Accrued or Carryover Hours for Employees

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/PTO_Register_Adjustment.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/PTO_Register_Adjustment.htm

Adjusting Accrued or Carryover Hours for Employees

You can use the Employee Time Off Register Adjustment form to adjust an employee’s number of accrued hours or carryover hours from a previous year. You can also adjust the accrued through date.

Note: Note the following when using the Employee Time Off Register Adjustment form:

  • Absence Codes must exist before you can use the Employee Time Off Register Adjustment form. See Creating Time Off Absence Codes for more information.

  • When you archive a register, you cannot adjust it. Therefore, to prevent you from making an adjustment to an archived register, these accrual schedules do not display as an option in the Accrual Schedule field.

[Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: The system automatically assigns the Journal ID.

In addition, you can access the Time Off Register Adjustment Import form by clicking the Import icon (

[Image: screenshot]

) that displays in this form. See Importing Time Off Adjustment Information for more information.

To adjust an employee’s number of hours, do the following:

  1. Enter the Employee by either entering the name, Social Security Number, or ID to display a pop-up list of matching employee records. You can also click the field label or press Ctrl+Enter with your cursor in this field to open the search window.

  2. Select the Time Off Accrual Schedule for this adjustment.

  3. Enter the Effective Date, which is the day of the adjustment or the start date of the adjustment period if the adjustment spans multiple days.

  4. Enter the Calculated Hours to add to the employee’s Time Off register.

  5. Enter the Carry-Over Hours to add to the employee’s Time Off register.

  6. Enter the Calculated Thru Date, which is the date through which you want to update the employee’s accrued through for the accrual schedule. Further accruals occur for payrolls with a pay date after this date. If you want to adjust only the date, enter zeroes in the hours fields.

  7. Enter a Comment about the adjustment, if applicable. For example, you can use this reference a specific voucher or event.

  8. Click Save.

Saving the Spreadsheet as a Template

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Save%20the%20Spreadsheet%20as%20a%20Template.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Save the Spreadsheet as a Template.htm

Saving the Spreadsheet as a Template

You can save specified fields into a template to provide faster selection when you need to import future field updates. Only selected fields are saved as a template.

To save selected fields as a template (assuming Generate Import Spreadsheet panel is expanded):

  1. Enter fields in the Fields to Update section.

  2. Enter a Template ID.

  3. Enter a descriptive name in the Template Description field.

  4. Click Save Template. If you make changes to an existing template and try to save it, the system asks if you want to write over the existing template. [Image: screenshot]

Note: Note: Saved templates will appear as selections in the Use Template drop-down under Generate Import Spreadsheet.

Assigning School District GeoCodes

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/School_District_Codes.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/School_District_Codes.htm

Assigning School District GeoCodes

You can assign a School District GeoCode in Employee Details in PrismHR to ensure that the system properly calculates the employee’s school district taxes.

  1. Refer to the Vertex Calculation Guide in the Customer Resource Center to determine the school district GeoCode for the appropriate state, town and school district for the selected employee.

  2. Open Employee Details (found in the HR menu under HR|Change.

  3. Enter the Employee ID and then select the Tax tab.

  4. Scroll down to the Miscellaneous panel and enter the School District GeoCode determined in step 1.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. Click Save.

Changing Shift Updates for Employees

You can make changes to an employee’s shift information using the Shift History Adjustment form. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To change an employee’s shift information:

  1. Enter or select the Employee ID. The shift history displays.

  2. Select a shift history record to delete or update in the Date Effective column. The selected row is highlighted and the Delete Date and Change Date buttons display.

  3. Select Delete Date to delete the shift information or Change Date to update the shift information.

Note: If an adjustment for the selected record has already been posted, you cannot delete or change the date.

Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance Audit

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Terminated%20Take%20On%20Employee%20Maintenance%20Audit.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance Audit.htm

Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance Audit

Use this option to view the audit logs, to view the changes made to a terminated take on employee record, using the Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance form.

Note: Note:

  • Worksite Managers, Worksite Trusted Advisors and Service Providers can access this option

  • This process uses Client Access Group and employee-level security

To view the audit log:

  1. On the Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance form, click Employee ID to select the employee for whom you want to view the audit log. The other fields on the form auto populates with the information available in the system.

  2. Click [Image: screenshot] (Action Menu) on the top right.

  3. Select Audit (first option in the drop-down) - the Audit Log Display opens in a separate window.

  4. Click a Log ID to view the respective changes made - the first Log ID from the top displays the latest change made.

  5. Click Close to close the Audit Log Display window.

Each Log ID displays the following information:

  • Audit Log Number shows the Log ID.

  • File Name shows the

  • Record ID shows the Employee ID of the employee whose record has changed.

  • Field Name shows the field where the change was made.

  • Before Value shows the value before changes were made.

  • After Value shows the changed value.

  • Type of Change shows how it changed example (AMENDED)

Controlling Field Value

  • Update Date shows the date on which the record was updated.

  • Update Time shows the time when the record was updated.

  • User ID shows the user ID used to change the record.

  • Account shows the user account used to change the record.

  • Client IP Address shows the Ip address used to change the record.

Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Change/Terminated%20Take%20On%20Employee%20Maintenance.htm Path: PD/HR/Change/Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance.htm

Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance

Use this form to update the employee information that were created using the Terminated Take On Employee Entry form. Other processes such as Employment Information Report (EEO-1) also utilize this information.

Note: Note: This process uses Client Access Group and employee-level security.

To access the form, click HR. Under HR|Change click Terminated Take On Employee Maintenance.

To update the information of the terminated take on employee:

  1. Complete the following fields:

In this field — Enter this

Employee ID — The employee ID of the terminated take on employee or click the Employee ID link to select the employee.

Social Security Number — Auto populates when a valid Employee ID is entered. Only the last four digits of the Social Security Number are displayed while the others are masked. Click the Edit SSN link to edit the Social Security Number.

First Name — The first name of the employee.

Middle Name — The middle name of the employee. This field is optional.

Last Name — The last name of the employee.

Birth Date — The date of birth of the employee. This field is optional.

Address — The street address of the employee.

Address Line 2 — Additional address information of the employee, for example, apartment number. This field is optional.

City — The name of the city of the employee.

State — Auto populates when a valid five digit zip code is entered in the Zip Code field.

Zip Code — The five digit zip code of the employee here.

Employer — The employer code of the employee or click the Employer link to select the employer code.

Employment Status — The employment status code of the employee or click the Employment Status link to select the employment status code.

Employment Type — The employment type code of the employee or click the Employment Type link to select the employment type code.

Position — The position code of the employee or click the Position link to select the position code.

Worksite Location — The worksite location code of the employee or click the Worksite Location link to select the worksite location code.

Department — The department code of the employee or click the Department link to select the department code. This field is optional.

Project/Cost Center — The project/cost center code of the employee or click the Project/Cost Center link to select the project/cost center code. This field is optional.

Benefit Group — The benefit group code of the employee of click the Benefit Group link to select the benefit group code. This field is optional.

  1. Click Save to update the information for the terminated employee. The screen is refreshed.

  2. Click Tax Register Report to generate a Tax Register Summary for the terminated employee containing SUTA and MISC taxes that are client reporting, provided the taxes have been entered in the employee record via a tax adjustment.

  3. Click the XLS link to download the summary.

Employee Retirement Loans Detail Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Reports/2020/Employee_Retirement_Loans_Detail_Report.htm Path: PD/HR/Reports/2020/Employee_Retirement_Loans_Detail_Report.htm

Employee Retirement Loans Detail Report

You can run a report linked to a loan number or a payroll number for each 401K loan for employees who have more than one 401K loan payment.

Using the Employee Retirement Loans Detail Report from the HR|Report menu, you can run reports that separate the loan payments instead of combining multiple payments into one deduction.

[Image: screenshot]

To run this report, do the following:

  1. Click theReports menu icon.

  2. Select Employee Retirement Loans Detail Report from the HR|Report menu.

  3. Select or enter the Loan Number or Payroll Number. If you select one of the fields, a form displays where you can select a number from the list or refine the search.

  4. Click Run. The Employee Retirement Loans Detail Report displays, listing the payment amount deducted per payroll for the employee’s 401K loan.

  5. To download the report, click XLS.

You can run a report, E-Verification Update Report, to list all employees who were submitted for e-verification and their e-verification status.

  3. Select or enter the Client ID. The Client Name displays.

  4. Enter the Start Date and End Date.

  5. Select Exclude Cases Closed, if appropriate.

  6. Click Run. The E-Verification Update Report displays, listing any employees who were submitted for e-verification during that time period.

  7. To download the report, click XLS.

Benefits Billing Detail Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Reports/Benefits_Billing_Report.htm Path: PD/HR/Reports/Benefits_Billing_Report.htm

Benefits Billing Detail Report

Use the Benefits Billing Detail Report form to determine what information prints on the Benefits Billing Detail Report and in what order.

[Image: screenshot]

  1. In the Selection Parameters section choose from the Defined Ranges drop-down to determine what displays on the report.

The drop-down contains the following options:

  • Current Month (default)- The date range is displayed as the first day of current month and the last day of current month. For example, if the current system date is 6/15/2024, then the range definition for this selection will be 6/1/2024-6/30/2024.

  • Year-to-date- The date range is displayed as the first day of the current year till the current system date. For example, if the current system date is 6/15/2024, then the range definition displayed on this selection will be 1/1/2024-6/15/2024.

  • Last Batch- The value displayed will be the date on which last batch was processed.

  • Prior Month- The date range is displayed as the first day of the previous month and the last day of the previous month. For example, if the current system date is 6/15/2024, then the range definition for this selection will be 5/1/2024-5/31/2024.

  • Prior 2 Months- The date range is displayed as the first day of the previous two months and the last day of the previous month. For example, if the current system date is 6/15/2024, then the range definition for this selection will be 4/1/2024-5/31/2024.

  • Prior 3 Months- The date range is displayed as the first day of the previous three months and the last day of the previous month. For example, if the current system date is 6/15/2024, then the range definition for this selection will be 3/1/2024-5/31/2024.

  • Prior Year- The date range is displayed as the first day of the previous year and the last day of the previous year. For example, if the current system date is 6/15/2024, then the range definition for this selection will be 1/1/2023-12/31/2023.

  • Custom Date Range- You can choose dates in the Pay Date On Or After and the Pay Date On Or Before fields.

  • Enter the Pay Date On or After and the Pay Date On or Before to run this report for a date range.

  • Select Batch ID- You can select the Payroll Number.

  1. Select options under the Sort Criteria section for how the report displays.

This section has the following fields:

  • Primary Sort- This selector indicates the top-level of sort organization for the report results.

  • Secondary Sort- This selector indicates the order of sort organization within the Primary Sort and the order of the column(s) following the Primary Sort column. For example, if you select Benefit Plan as the Primary Sort option and Employee as the Secondary Sort option, then the results displayed will be first by Plan ID and then by Employee ID within the respective Plan ID result segments.

  • Report Type- This indicates whether you want a detailed or a summarized report.

  • Use ‘XLS’ Format- Select this checkbox if you want the report in an XLS format.

  1. Enter the report filters to display on the report. Enter the field type. The field label displays. For example, if you enter a Location, the Location Name displays. The report displays records for that Location.

  2. Click Run.

[Image: screenshot]

Depending on the Sort Criteria option selected, the possible report columns that display are:

  • Location: The benefits plan location.

  • Department: The benefits plan department.

  • Benefit Plan ID: The benefit plan ID.

  • Insurance Class: The Insurance Class to which the Benefit Plan is associated with.

  • Division: The benefits plan division.

  • Emp. ID: The employee ID.

  • Employee Name: Employee name displays as last name first.

  • Plan Description: The benefits plan description.

  • Plan Id: The benefits plan ID.

  • Voucher#: The plan voucher of the benefit plan.

  • Pay Date: The date paid.

  • From Arrears: The amount from arrears, if any.

  • Amount Billed: The amount billed to the client.

  • Employee Deduction: The amount deducted from the employee during that date range.

  • Employee Contribution: The amount the employee contributed during that date range.

  • Net-Amount Billed: The amount billed to the client minus the employee contribution and arrears.

Employee Hours by Active Weeks Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Reports/EE-Hours-Worked-Repo.htm Path: PD/HR/Reports/EE-Hours-Worked-Repo.htm

Employee Hours by Active Weeks Report

The Employee Hours By Weeks Report enables you to run a report identifying employee hours worked by active days and weeks.

Note: Terminated employees do not display in the report unless they were active during the date range set in the Selection Parameters panel.

To run the report:

  1. In the Starting Date field, enter the first date to be included in the report. This field is required.

  2. In the Ending Date field, enter the last date to be included in the report. This field is required.

You can filter the report by:

  • Employee ID

  • Division

  • Department

  • Location

  • Project

  • Position

  • Shift

  1. Select Run. The report displays.

Viewing the Report

The following fields display on the report:

Field Description
Employee Name The name of the employee.
Employee ID The employee ID number.
Hire Date The date the employee was hired.
Pay Method The method by which the employee is paid: - H: Hourly - S: Salary - VSalary: Variable Salary Note: Employees assigned the Variable Salary option in the Pay Method field on the Employee Details form displays as “VSalary” in the on-screen and .xls report output. (The Variable Salary option only displays if the VARSALMETHOD custom feature code is enabled on the System Parameters form.)
  • H: Hourly
  • S: Salary
  • VSalary: Variable Salary | Location | The worksite location code. | | Division | The division code. | | Dept | The department code. | | Position | The position code. | | Work State | The employee’s current work state. | | Hours Worked | The number of hours the employee worked. | | Active Days | The number of days within the selected date range that the employe was active. | | Avg Per Week | The average number of hours worked per week based on active days and hours worked. | ========================================================================

Emergency Contact Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Reports/Emergency_Contact_Report.htm Path: PD/HR/Reports/Emergency_Contact_Report.htm

Emergency Contact Report

This report provides a listing of employees’ emergency contact information. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

To run the Emergency Contact Report:

  1. Enter the Selection Parameters to determine what displays on the report. If left blank, all employees display:

  • Select the Status Class of the employee (for example, Active, On Leave, or Terminated).

  • Select the Type Class of the employee (for example, Full-Time or Part-Time).

  1. Enter the Sort Parameters.
  • Select Primary Sort to sort by Employee Name, or Employee ID.
  1. Enter the Report Filters to display on the report:
  • Enter the field type. The field label displays. For example, if you enter a Location, the Location Name displays. The report displays records for that Location.
  1. Click Run. The report output displays. For example:

[Image: screenshot]

The report columns are:

  • Employee Name: Employee name displays as last name first.

  • Employee ID: Employee ID.

  • Contact Name: The name of the employee’s emergency contact.

  • Contact Relation: The relationship between the employee and emergency contact.

  • Contact Information: The telephone number, email, address, or other way to contact the emergency contact.

  • Type: The type of contact information entered for the emergency contact (for example PHONE, EMAIL, or ADDRESS).

  • Contact If Arrested/Detained: Indicates whether the employee’s emergency contact will be contacted in case of the employee’s arrest/detainment on the worksite. (This option is set in the Contact if arrested/detained? field on the Personal tab in the Employee Details form.)

Employee Count Analysis Report

Source: https://tech-docs.prismhr.com/SP_help/Content/PD/HR/Reports/Employee%20Count%20Analysis.htm Path: PD/HR/Reports/Employee Count Analysis.htm

Analyzing the Employee Count

This utility provides a count of employees, either by the number of paid employees or by the classification status of employees in the system.

Note: Note: Client Access Group Security is enabled on this form.

To access the form, click

[Image: screenshot]

(Back Office). Under Operations|Report, click Employee Count Analysis.

To generate an employee count analysis:

  1. In the Employer ID field enter the Employer ID, or click the Employer ID link and select the employer to generate the employee count for that Employer. If left blank, and the Client ID is also blank, it will generate employee count of all employees in the system.

  2. In the Client ID field enter the Client ID, or click the Client ID link and select the client to generate the employee count for that Client.

  3. In the As of Date field, enter the date for which you want the Employee Count Analysis.

  4. For Count Type, select any one of the following two options:

  • Count Paid Employees - gives you a count of employees who were paid using the system as per Employee Type selected.

  • Employee Status Classification - gives you a breakup of the count of employees as per various classification in the system as per Employee Type selected.

  1. If Employee Status Classification is selected, then select any one of the following options:

  • All

  • Active

  • Terminated

  • Leave of Absence

  1. For the Employee Type, select any one of the following options:

  • All

  • Full Time

  • Part Time

  • Independent Contractor

  1. Click Count Employees - a new window Calculating Employee Count appears.

  2. Scroll down the window to view the number of employees counted.

  3. Click Close to close the Calculating Employee Count window.
